# Features, with honest status

Each row is a feature Synthyrion Core L5 sets out to have, and where it stands. The table below is generated from the project’s own equivalence table; it is not edited by hand, so it cannot drift from the truth.

**Status.** Synthyrion Core L5 is a capability-based microkernel written in Rust. Its primary target is x86-64. AArch32, AArch64 and RISC-V 64 ports run in emulation (QEMU) only. It is under active development. Its feature status is published on this site, with the strength of each piece of evidence (tested / bounded / panic-free / functionally proven; host, virtual machine or hardware). **It has no safety, security or government certification. There is no whole-kernel functional-correctness proof. It is not FIPS 140-3 validated.**

**How to read the status.** Done the feature exists and a test or proof row checks it; the strength of that evidence (host, virtual machine or hardware; tested, bounded, panic-free or functionally proven) is in the row. Done, limit stated submitted as complete with the limit the row states. In progress part exists, and the gap is named. Planned not started. “Different design” means the guarantee is provided by a mechanism of Synthyrion’s own, described in the row.

A Done row is not a functional-correctness proof. The strength of the checking behind each row is on the [assurance goals page](https://synthyrion.com/core/proofs.html).

Authority is a sealed value the holder carries, not a slot the kernel holds; Cap::new is public and unauthenticated (synthyrion_caps/src/lib.rs:71), so safety rests on always sealing…

### Gap or limit, in full

Authority is a sealed value the holder carries, not a slot the kernel holds; `Cap::new` is public and unauthenticated (`synthyrion_caps/src/lib.rs:71`), so safety rests on always sealing **2026-11-04, spec level:** authority cannot be amplified down the capability tree: `inv_mono` (`inv_mono_derive`, `inv_mono_move`, `inv_mono_delete`, `inv_mono_revoke`, `inv_mono_retype`, `step_inv_mono`, `executions_keep_inv_mono`, `boot_executions_keep_inv_mono`, theory `Synthyrion_CapMono`) says every derivation edge joins a child capability that is at most its parent (same object, rights only shrink); it is preserved by all 14 user operations and so by every execution from boot (plant `plant_mono_convicted`, session `SYNTHYRION_Plant_CapMono_Attempt` FAILS). Strength per theorem: UNBOUNDED, SPEC LEVEL: Isabelle `SYNTHYRION_Core` (`proofs/isabelle/`, 42 theories, no `sorry`/`oops`/`axiomatization`), every state of the abstract specification and every execution length; NOT a code-level refinement: the Rust kernel is not proved to refine that specification, and the bounded refinements V-551 / V-561 over the real kernel are unchanged (`docs/proofs/ISABELLE_PROGRESS.md`, rows I8-I11, unit proof_cspace 22300). The Rust capability store is not covered by it. **2026-11-10, spec level (unit proof_cspace2 89100, 223089626):** the kernel as it is on main (rights on the pointer path per `cs_resolve_need`, the untyped-with-children refusal in `derive_ids`) is modelled in Isabelle theory `Synthyrion_CSpaceK` (`proofs/isabelle/cspacek/Synthyrion_CSpaceK.thy`, session `SYNTHYRION_CSpaceK`, 33 lemmas and theorems, no `sorry`, raw log `docs/proofs/ISABELLE_BUILD_LOG_2026-11-10_CSPACEK.txt`). Theorems `copy_needs_path_rights`, `mint_needs_path_rights`, `move_needs_path_rights`, `mutate_needs_path_rights`, `delete_needs_path_rights`, `revoke_needs_path_rights`: a missing right on the CNode capability of the path is Denied before anything else (plant: session `SYNTHYRION_Plant_CSpaceKRights_Attempt` FAILS). Strength: model proved in Isabelle (UNBOUNDED, SPEC LEVEL); the spec-to-code link is by reading plus the kernel tests (`tests/cspace_authority.rs`), NOT a refinement. Not modelled: the sibling ring order (F13), `node_room` and the object-memory refusals, queued destruction and the revoke budget, retype (untyped state is an oracle), the packed locator, and non-Memory capabilities naming a region.

Status word unchanged.

Revoke does not block new derivations between steps (a step finding nothing is Done); the 64-slot epoch table of the sealed-value path still over-revokes (section B0, finding) 2026-11-04…

### Gap or limit, in full

Revoke does not block new derivations between steps (a step finding nothing is `Done`); the 64-slot epoch table of the sealed-value path still over-revokes (section B0, finding) **2026-11-04, spec level:** revoke: `revoke_exact` (exactly the descendants of the slot are emptied and unlinked, nothing else changes), `revoke_complete` (afterwards the slot has no descendants), `revoke_idempotent` (a second revoke is a no-op) and `revoke_keeps_target` (the revoked slot keeps its capability and its place); delete: `delete_exact` (empties exactly one slot, touches no other capability, nothing points at it), `delete_ancestry` and `delete_descendants` (the ancestor relation among all OTHER slots is exactly the old one, children re-parented, `descendants s' a = descendants s a - {sl}`: deleting a middle capability cannot launder a derived capability out of an ancestor's later revoke) (`Synthyrion_CapPost`); plants `plant_revoke_convicted` (revokes only direct children) and `plant_delete_convicted` (orphans the children), sessions `SYNTHYRION_Plant_CapRevoke_Attempt` and `SYNTHYRION_Plant_CapDelete_Attempt` FAIL. Strength per theorem: UNBOUNDED, SPEC LEVEL: Isabelle `SYNTHYRION_Core` (`proofs/isabelle/`, 42 theories, no `sorry`/`oops`/`axiomatization`), every state of the abstract specification and every execution length; NOT a code-level refinement: the Rust kernel is not proved to refine that specification, and the bounded refinements V-551 / V-561 over the real kernel are unchanged (`docs/proofs/ISABELLE_PROGRESS.md`, rows I8-I11, unit proof_cspace 22300). The object finalisation a delete may trigger is not in the spec.

2026-11-01 (work stream F21/F8; units f8_ipcbuf, f8_vspace_bind, f8_gate_rows) - the two named gaps are closed and the row moves PARTIAL to HAVE.

### Gap or limit, in full

**2026-11-01 (work stream F21/F8; units `f8_ipcbuf`, `f8_vspace_bind`, `f8_gate_rows`) - the two named gaps are closed and the row moves PARTIAL to HAVE.** **IPC-buffer binding:** `Kernel::tcb_set_ipc_buffer` / `cs_tcb_set_ipc_buffer` / `ipc_buffer_of` (`microkernel/src/tcb.rs`; WRITE on the Task capability): the address is `IPC_BUFFER_BYTES` aligned, the whole buffer lies inside ONE range the task has mapped, `0` unbinds, a task awaiting placement is refused, a refusal changes nothing, and `ipc_buffer_of` re-validates against the live mappings on every read so an `Unmap` of the backing page removes the binding; `tests/tcb_ipcbuf.rs` 5 tests, plants `plant_tcb_ipcbuf_unaligned`, `plant_tcb_ipcbuf_unbacked`, `plant_tcb_ipcbuf_stale`, `plant_tcb_nogate` each RED. Synthyrion's messages are registers plus the inbox, so no message is copied through the buffer: the kernel records and validates the binding. **VSpace bind BY CAPABILITY**: `Kernel::cs_tcb_bind_vspace` (`microkernel/src/tcb_vspace.rs`) takes two pointers in the CALLER's own CSpace, the target Task capability (WRITE) and the donor Task capability (WRITE; a retyped Task capability is born without GRANT) whose address space the target will run in. Refused, changing nothing: a pointer that holds no such capability, another kind, or missing WRITE (`Denied`: a root is never named by a number); a donor that is not a task retyped out of an untyped region, whose space is the kernel's, or whose root names no page-table tree this kernel holds (`Invalid`: no forged root); a donor whose scheduling partition is not its space's (the space is bound to another partition) or a target already overridden into another partition (`Denied`); a bind that would put an armed, unscheduled kernel in a second partition (`Denied`); a target that is not fresh and unplaced (`Invalid`: a bind configures a fresh TCB, it is not a move). On success the target is placed exactly as `place_authorized` places a task and cannot be admitted again. `tests/tcb_vspace.rs` 4 tests, plants `plant_vsbind_not_held`, `plant_vsbind_cross_partition`, `plant_vsbind_forged_root` each RED. F24's `task_set_vspace` / `vspace_map_frame` rows were not on main when this was built; the bind reads the donor's `aspace_root`, so it applies unchanged to a root that path derives. **Gate rows 144 `tcb_set_ipc_buffer` and 145 `tcb_bind_vspace`** (`gate_taskcfg.rs`, `synthyrion_abi::def`, regenerated `synthyrion_client` and C header, golden rows appended): an empty slot is `LOOKUP`, another kind or a missing right `DENIED`, a bad buffer or a bad donor `INVALID`; `synthyrion_gatecases` success scripts and 9 refusal scripts run by the table test; plants `plant_gate_f8_dropped` (the rows answer NOT_WIRED) and `plant_gate_f8_lookup` (a failed lookup is answered DENIED) each RED. VM strength (QEMU TCG, ring 3), see the strength cell. The donor needs WRITE, not GRANT, because a retyped Task capability is born READ and WRITE only (`RetypeKind::Task` in `objects.rs`): requiring GRANT would make the bind unusable by the very task that retyped the donor, and the retyper owns the space it made. **Earlier status (kept):** **By pointer (2026-10-23):** `cs_tcb_suspend`, `cs_tcb_resume`, `cs_tcb_read_registers`, `cs_tcb_write_registers`, `cs_tcb_configure`, `cs_tcb_set_priority` and `cs_tcb_set_mcp` take `(cptr, depth)` in the CALLER's own CSpace and need a Task capability with the right (WRITE; READ to read registers), refusing a non-resolving pointer, an empty slot, another kind of capability or a missing right with `Denied` and no change; they share their bodies with the sealed calls, the caller is the configure authority (its own MCP bounds priority and MCP), the fault handler is an Endpoint capability named by a pointer, and a task retyped BY a task (`cs_retype`) is born in the retyper's scheduling partition; `tests/tcb_ptr.rs` 4 tests, 9 plants RED. **Refreshed 2026-10-31 (ROWS work stream, main `5a3868e7e`):** the selector rows are WIRED now, not Defined: the kernel gate serves the TCB and scheduling family 32, 33, 44-47, 51-53, 56-59, 82-86, 99, 100 (`gate/tcb_rows.rs`, `860213955`; ring 3 on the booted image under QEMU TCG, VM, as measured by that work stream), the timeout-handler rows 102/103 (F10, host) and `task_admit` 140 / `task_set_cspace` 141 (`gate_taskcfg.rs`, host; see F24). WAS STILL (closed 2026-11-01, see above): a task has no IPC-buffer binding object (`grep -rni` for ipc_buffer or ipcbuf over `microkernel/src` finds nothing; Synthyrion's messages are registers plus the inbox, a different design that the owner has not ruled DIFFERENT-DESIGN) and `tcb_configure` sets priority, MCP, fault handler and weight only, not a VSpace binding (B5 remainder). Was: (2026-10-23) the selector rows were Defined, not wired to a trap; and operations were model methods with no register-context object

Donation over call/reply and passive servers are built (F10 MCS 2/n: ipcx.rs sc_donate_pending, objects.rs sc_donate/sc_return/sc_settle_retiring/set_task_passive, ABI row 99; tests…

### Gap or limit, in full

Donation over call/reply and passive servers are built (F10 MCS 2/n: `ipcx.rs` `sc_donate_pending`, `objects.rs` `sc_donate`/`sc_return`/`sc_settle_retiring`/`set_task_passive`, ABI row 99; tests `mcs_donation.rs`, plants `plants_mcs2.py`); the proved sporadic refill queue (`synthyrion_sched_rt::sporadic`) is wired into every context (F10 MCS 3/n: `objects.rs` `Sc::queue`/`set_queue`, tests `mcs_sporadic.rs`, plants `plants_mcs3.py`), so a context can no longer run twice its budget across a window boundary; **priority inheritance over call is built (F10 PI, 2026-10-29)**: the priority tier (`objects.rs` `pick_priority`, on the global and the partitioned dispatch paths) orders by the EFFECTIVE priority, the highest of a task's own and that of every task blocked in a call to it, transitively through servers that are themselves blocked calling further, and only inside one partition; a best-effort server joins the tier while it serves a prioritised client; the loan ends with the answer; notification-side donation is built (F10 MCS 4/n: `objects.rs` `bind_sched_context_notification`, `sc_lend_notification`, `sc_release_notification`; ABI row 100; tests `mcs_notif.rs`, plants `plants_mcs4.py`): a passive task a signal wakes is lent the notification's context until it waits again. Now, once object creation is sealed, a retype (`retype`, `cs_retype`, gate selector 51) makes only an UNCONFIGURED context (`{budget: 0, period: 0}`: it grants no time; any other budget is `Denied` and charges nothing), and the one way to give a context time is `configure_sched_context(ctl, sc, budget, period)` / `cs_configure_sched_context` / gate selector 101 `configure_sched_context(sc_cptr, budget, period)`, which needs the schedule authority with WRITE (this kernel's SchedControl, the capability that already gated the heap `create_sched_context`); the refills restart full, the binding is kept. plants `plants_sched_control.py` 4 of 4 RED; HOST only. Before the seal (the boot) a retype may still carry a budget, as every heap creator may. (2) ~~timeout faults~~ **BUILT (F10 timeout faults, 2026-10-29)**: a task may name a TIMEOUT HANDLER; every dispatch path charges through `sc_charge_task`, and when a dispatch spends the last of the budget the next scheduling decision (`deliver_timeout_fault`, first act of `schedule`, `schedule_weighted`, `schedule_2tier`, `schedule_partitioned`) suspends the task and reports `FaultKind::Timeout` (code 8; addr = the context, ip = its budget) to the timeout handler through the fault reply-slot path (never the fault handler, never the inbox), unless the budget came back meanwhile; the reply or a restart ends the suspension and grants no time; without a live timeout handler the task is only throttled, never killed. Tests `timeout_fault.rs` (3), `gate_tcb.rs` (+1, rows 102 / 103), `synthyrion_gatecases` scripts; plants `plants_timeout_fault.py` 4 of 4 RED; HOST only; (3) ~~`SchedContext_Consumed`~~ **BUILT (F10 consumed time, 2026-10-30)**: a context counts the dispatches charged to it (a guarded scalar, self-heal word 2), and `sched_context_consumed` / gate selector 104 (the context capability with WRITE) returns the count since the last read and resets it; tests `sched_consumed.rs` (1), `gate_tcb.rs` (+1), `selfheal_aux.rs` now 7 guarded fields; plants `plants_sched_consumed.py` 4 of 4 RED; HOST only; (4) ~~`SchedContext_YieldTo`~~ **BUILT (F10 yield-to, 2026-10-30)**: `sched_context_yield_to(caller, sc)` / gate selector 105 (the context capability with WRITE; the caller yields): the task running on that context goes to the HEAD of its priority (the next priority-tier decision runs it first among the tasks at the priority being served, never ahead of a higher priority; one-shot), only if it is in the priority tier (else `Invalid`), at most the caller's MCP and in the caller's scheduling partition (else `Denied`); tests `sched_yield_to.rs` (3), `gate_tcb.rs` row 105, `synthyrion_gatecases` script; plants `plants_sched_yield_to.py` 4 of 4 RED; HOST only; (5) ~~per-core SchedControl / core affinity~~ **BUILT (F10 per-core SchedControl, 2026-10-30)**: `configure_sched_context_on(ctl, sc, core, budget, period)` and gate selector 101's fourth argument place a context on ONE core (a core the kernel does not schedule is `Invalid`; the plain `configure_sched_context` is core 0); `dispatch_core` makes its decision for that core (`KernelO::cur_core`), and `sc_ok` refuses a task whose context lives on another core, so the budget is spent where it was granted; a boot context (given its budget before the seal) has no core and runs anywhere; the core is a guarded scalar (self-heal word 3). Tests `sched_core_affinity.rs` (2, two cores in the SMP model), `gate_tcb.rs` (+1), `selfheal_aux.rs` 8 guarded fields; plants `plants_sched_core_affinity.py` 4 of 4 RED; HOST only (no two-core VM run). Order of work (project lead 2026-10-29): (1), (2), (3), (4), then (5), each with plants **2026-11-10, spec level (scheduling-context donation in the combined non-interference state):** `Synthyrion_NIScK` (9f9c139c5, a queued submission, session SYNTHYRION_NIScK on SYNTHYRION_NICapXK; no sorry; raw log docs/proofs/ISABELLE_BUILD_LOG_2026-11-10_NISCK.txt). KERNEL PATH MODELLED (read from the source): DONATION OVER CALL `sc_donate_pending` (ipcx.rs:880-899, run at the end of a call ipcx.rs:872 and after a reply ipcx.rs:1054): a server that runs on no scheduling context gets the context of the first queued call whose caller runs on one (`sc_donate` objects.rs:1534-1551: the caller's binding is removed, the server's made, the holder becomes the server) and the call's reply slot records it (`ReplySlot.donated`, ipcx.rs:500); RETURN `sc_return` (objects.rs:1564-1620, from a reply ipcx.rs:1046-1047): when the answered caller is the context's owner the context is taken from WHOEVER holds it, otherwise it acts only while the answering server still holds it; it goes to the caller if the caller runs on none, else to the owner, else it is left unbound. STATE: the context each task runs on, the holder of each context and the context each outstanding call donated are added to the observed state (a context is identified by its owner task); the actions are the donation and the return, which read the server's inbox and the caller's reply slot of the combined state and write only the three new fields. INVARIANT (authority only along authorised edges): every task runs on a context its owner is authorised with, every held context is held by a task authorised with its owner, every donated call names such a context (authorised = flow both ways, the call authority). THEOREM `xsc_noninterference` over every earlier action kind (send, receive, signal, wait, call, reply, collect, fault, restart, receive-call, exit, the six IRQ actions, capability transfer) plus the donation and the return; also `donation_moves_the_context`, `donation_needs_a_free_server`, `return_gives_the_context_back`, `sret_pass` (a return by a server that no longer holds the context leaves the contexts alone). STRENGTH: UNBOUNDED, MODEL LEVEL, not a refinement. POLICY ANSWER (finding): donation gives flow both ways between the client and the server, but that edge ALREADY exists (the call authority); it adds NO information-flow edge, and needs no new policy input. The contexts a call chain donates stay inside ONE authorisation clique (client, server, the servers it calls in turn, the owner), so a return, which acts on whoever holds the context, writes only tasks its domain already flows to. NOT MODELLED, STATED PLAINLY: the BUDGET. The server runs on, is charged to and is throttled by the client's budget, so the client's remaining budget and throttling reveal how long the server ran: a TIMING channel from server to client along an authorised edge. The model has no budget and no time, so it says nothing about it; a policy that wants no timing channel from a server to its client must not let that client donate to that server. PLANTS convicted on a concrete state (green convict session) with Attempt sessions that FAIL: a donation to a server that already runs on a context (its own is lost), a return that does not check the holder (it steals the context from the task that holds it now), a donation with no queued call (it takes the context of a domain the server cannot flow to). MODEL CHOICES (disclosed): the donation and the return are separate actions attributed to the server's and the answering task's domain, where the kernel runs them inside call and reply (more interleavings; the kernel's composite step is covered because caller and server are in one authorisation clique; not mechanised as its own theorem); the model lets the action name any donatable caller where the kernel picks the first. NOT covered (stated): budget and time, the abort and exit return of a donated context (ipcx.rs:1387, 2360), creation, binding and destruction of contexts and the owner-cleared branch of `sc_return`, timing channels, any code-level refinement.

Install and reset work; moving a task is F12.

### Gap or limit, in full

Install and reset work; moving a task is F12. ** (2026-10-20): non-interference holds ONLY in the partitioned mode.** Without a schedule the dispatcher is the global `schedule_2tier`, which leaks timing between partitions. `Kernel::domain_isolation` reports `Partitioned` / `SingleDomain` / `Unprotected`; `arm_domain_isolation` (schedule authority, one-way) makes `Unprotected` unreachable (a second partition is refused while no schedule is installed, and a separating schedule cannot be reset). **Boot arms it (2026-10-21):** `boot_root_task` arms domain isolation as its last fallible step, with the schedule authority it hands to the root task, and REFUSES (`BootError::Unprotected`, nothing carved) a boot that would start an unscheduled kernel with tasks of two partitions; tests `the_boot_arms_domain_isolation_so_a_second_partition_needs_a_schedule` and `a_boot_that_would_start_unprotected_is_refused_and_changes_nothing`, plants `plant_boot_unarmed` / `plant_boot_unprotected_ok`. **Host-proven in `boot_root_task`; in the UEFI image (2026-10-21, `boot_uefi --features bootarm_scene`, `proofs/bootarm_uefi_proof.py`, VM (QEMU TCG), not bare metal) the same function runs and the plants `bootarm_plant_unarmed` / `bootarm_plant_unprotected_ok` are convicted, and the live ring-3 kernel is itself built by `boot_root_task` (see F24); bare metal is unverified.** ** (2026-10-22, branch `time_prot_u1`):** the domain switch is the place the microarchitectural state is cleared; see section 3a.

Messages are one 64-bit word plus caps, not a register/buffer message 2026-11-04, spec level (IPC redesign, unit 9100): the Lean spec now models the kernel's INBOX IPC: send never blocks…

### Gap or limit, in full

Messages are one 64-bit word plus caps, not a register/buffer message **2026-11-04, spec level (IPC redesign, unit 9100):** the Lean spec now models the kernel's INBOX IPC: `send` never blocks, it appends to the target task's inbox through a WRITE endpoint capability (an endpoint capability names a TASK, not a queue object) and is refused with no transition when the inbox holds 32 messages (`INBOX_CAP`) or the sender already has 32 in flight (`MAX_OUTSTANDING_SENDS`); `recv` never blocks, it reads only the caller's OWN endpoint (READ), takes the OLDEST message, writes the message register and releases the sender's in-flight slot, and an empty inbox answers Empty and changes nothing. Theorems: `InboxInv`, `step_frame_inbox`, `inbox_step` (every transition keeps inbox <= 32 and in-flight <= 32), `send_needs_write_cap`, `recv_own_endpoint_only`, `send_never_blocks`, `recv_never_blocks`; plants IP1-IP9 each convicted (full inbox, full budget, foreign endpoint, no release, no WRITE, LIFO, off-by-one, the old blocking send, the old blocking recv). Under a one-way policy the in-flight count is a receiver-to-sender channel (finding, not yet a theorem). Strength per theorem: UNBOUNDED, SPEC LEVEL: Lean 4 (`proofs/lean/Synthyrion/Spec/Step.lean`, unit proof_mem_hw 37600), every state of the abstract specification and every execution length; NOT a code-level refinement: the Rust kernel is not proved to refine the specification (`docs/proofs/LEAN_PROGRESS.md`). NOT modelled yet: `call` / `reply` (blocking until the reply), messages that carry capabilities, the pending-fault and bound-notification priority inside `recv`, and the wake-up of the receiver. NON-INTERFERENCE OVER send / recv IS OPEN: the old synchronous-endpoint non-interference files (`NIE.lean` to `NIE9.lean`, the plants P30-P32) described a different kernel and are SUPERSEDED, kept in `proofs/lean/superseded/ipc_sync_endpoints/`; restating them over the inbox model (a send is visible to the target task's domain; the in-flight count is a flow from receiver to sender, a new two-way link) is the next proof unit. What the redesign did to the other theorems (from `LEAN_PROGRESS.md`): SURVIVE UNCHANGED `inv_step`, `integrity_user` (send/recv cases rewritten, statements the same), `inv_reach` and the capability, scheduler, notification, fault, IRQ, signal, wait, thread-scheduler and domain-switch non-interference; RESTATED the integrity of send/recv as `send_needs_write_cap` / `recv_own_endpoint_only`; DROPPED as describing a different kernel `ipc_ep_enqueue`, `ipc_ep_dequeue` and the endpoint-queue clauses of `IpcInv` (kept, vacuous). **2026-11-08 (owner decision, 2026-11-07, decision record):** one 64-bit word plus capabilities is ruled a DIFFERENT DESIGN, not a missing feature: bulk data moves through shared frames, and there will be NO four-word register messages. The gap named at the start of this cell (not a register or buffer message) is that design.

No authority or isolation is lost: (1) sending authority is the same (a capability per receiver, rights and badge fixed at mint, GRANT to pass it on); (2) receiving is STRICTER (only the…

### Gap or limit, in full

**No authority or isolation is lost:** (1) sending authority is the same (a capability per receiver, rights and badge fixed at mint, GRANT to pass it on); (2) receiving is STRICTER (only the owner, never a badged or reply capability); (3) nothing is shared, so there is no common queue to flood or starve a third party (the bound is per receiver and a full inbox refuses the SENDER); (4) the reply path is single-use and dies with its caller. **2026-11-04, spec level (IPC redesign, unit 9100):** the abstract specification now follows THIS design: the Lean spec now models the kernel's INBOX IPC: `send` never blocks, it appends to the target task's inbox through a WRITE endpoint capability (an endpoint capability names a TASK, not a queue object) and is refused with no transition when the inbox holds 32 messages (`INBOX_CAP`) or the sender already has 32 in flight (`MAX_OUTSTANDING_SENDS`); `recv` never blocks, it reads only the caller's OWN endpoint (READ), takes the OLDEST message, writes the message register and releases the sender's in-flight slot, and an empty inbox answers Empty and changes nothing. Theorems: `InboxInv`, `step_frame_inbox`, `inbox_step` (every transition keeps inbox <= 32 and in-flight <= 32), `send_needs_write_cap`, `recv_own_endpoint_only`, `send_never_blocks`, `recv_never_blocks`; plants IP1-IP9 each convicted (full inbox, full budget, foreign endpoint, no release, no WRITE, LIFO, off-by-one, the old blocking send, the old blocking recv). Under a one-way policy the in-flight count is a receiver-to-sender channel (finding, not yet a theorem). Strength per theorem: UNBOUNDED, SPEC LEVEL: Lean 4 (`proofs/lean/Synthyrion/Spec/Step.lean`, unit proof_mem_hw 37600), every state of the abstract specification and every execution length; NOT a code-level refinement: the Rust kernel is not proved to refine the specification (`docs/proofs/LEAN_PROGRESS.md`). NOT modelled yet: `call` / `reply` (blocking until the reply), messages that carry capabilities, the pending-fault and bound-notification priority inside `recv`, and the wake-up of the receiver. NON-INTERFERENCE OVER send / recv WAS OPEN ON THE LEAN SIDE (the Isabelle model below now proves it, 2026-11-10): the old synchronous-endpoint non-interference files (`NIE.lean` to `NIE9.lean`, the plants P30-P32) described a different kernel and are SUPERSEDED, kept in `proofs/lean/superseded/ipc_sync_endpoints/`; restating them over the inbox model (a send is visible to the target task's domain; the in-flight count is a flow from receiver to sender, a new two-way link) is the next proof unit. What the redesign did to the other theorems (from `LEAN_PROGRESS.md`): SURVIVE UNCHANGED `inv_step`, `integrity_user` (send/recv cases rewritten, statements the same), `inv_reach` and the capability, scheduler, notification, fault, IRQ, signal, wait, thread-scheduler and domain-switch non-interference; RESTATED the integrity of send/recv as `send_needs_write_cap` / `recv_own_endpoint_only`; DROPPED as describing a different kernel `ipc_ep_enqueue`, `ipc_ep_dequeue` and the endpoint-queue clauses of `IpcInv` (kept, vacuous). **2026-11-10, ISABELLE MODELS:** 2026-11-10, spec level, Isabelle/HOL (Isabelle2025-2 on the proof VM, no sorry, raw checker logs in docs/proofs/ISABELLE_BUILD_LOG_2026-11-10_INBOXK.txt, _INBOXK_EXIT.txt, _INTEGK.txt, _NIK.txt, and the full re-run ISABELLE_RERUN_MAIN_3ef2474bd_2026-11-10.txt). STRENGTH: UNBOUNDED, MODEL LEVEL: every state of the model and every run length; the models were WRITTEN FROM THE RUST SOURCE with the file:line of every rule in each theory header (send cspace.rs:940-956, lib.rs:1713-1749; recv lib.rs:1343-1364; exit lib.rs:1859-1880); it is NOT a refinement: the Rust kernel is not proved to implement the models, and nothing is run against the code by this proof. Session SYNTHYRION_InboxK, `proofs/isabelle/inboxk/`. (1) `Synthyrion_InboxK`: send never blocks, is refused with nothing changed at 32 outstanding sends or at 32 incoming deliveries / 64 slots, wakes the target unless held; recv reads only the caller's own endpoint, is Denied on a pending fault or a Rich front, pops the oldest, releases the sender's outstanding count, and an empty inbox changes nothing. Invariant `inv_k`, preserved by `send_inv` and `recv_inv`: per task outstanding <= 32, incoming <= 32, length <= 64, and outstanding = in-flight word messages + unread bounces. (2) `Synthyrion_InboxKExit`: `bounce_never_refused` (under the invariant the slot guard of `deliver` is never true for a bounce: the outstanding limit reserves the room, so the ignored refusal in `bounce_inbox_of` is dead code) and `exit_inv`. (3) `Synthyrion_IntegK`: `send_integrity` (only the sender row and the target row change), `recv_integrity` / `recv_integrity_authorised` (the caller and the task that sent the word read), with the invariant `inbox_auth` (a delivery from f sits in c's inbox only because f held endpoint WRITE to c) kept by send and recv. (4) `Synthyrion_NIK`: `nik_noninterference` with an explicit policy: security domains, a reflexive transitive flow relation, and the endpoint authority predicate; the ONE link from capabilities to flow is that an endpoint capability from a to t gives flow BOTH WAYS. For two start states that agree on every domain that may flow to u, running any list of send / recv actions gives u the same view as running only the actions of domains that may flow to u (own copy of the Rushby unwinding theorem with a state invariant and step consistency over the actor's sources). THE EARLIER FINDING (Lean, "the in-flight count is a receiver-to-sender channel") IS NOW A THEOREM-LEVEL FACT: plant NIKBackEdge shows that under a one-way policy a receive writes the sender's row, so the back edge is necessary. PLANTS, each convicted on a concrete state by a green convict session and by an Attempt session that FAILS: no outstanding refusal, no release on recv, a drop-on-exit (the V-560 defect), a send that also wipes a bystander inbox, a send that skips the capability check, a policy without the back edge. NOT covered (stated): call / reply and the one-shot reply capability, the rich receive (capabilities, the pending-fault and bound-notification priority inside recv: modelled only as Denied), capability transfer, the reply-slot and abort side of exit, timing channels and the scheduler, the sealed host API, and any code-level refinement. The OLDER Isabelle IPC and non-interference theorems (`Synthyrion_Ipc`, `kernel_noninterference2`) still describe synchronous blocking endpoints and still do NOT count for this row. **2026-11-10 COMPOSITION:** 2026-11-10 COMPOSITION, model level, Isabelle/HOL (no sorry, raw log docs/proofs/ISABELLE_BUILD_LOG_2026-11-10_NICOMPK.txt): the send / receive proof (`Synthyrion_NIK`) and the signal / wait proof (`Synthyrion_NINtfnK`) are now ONE theorem over ONE state: `ucomp_noninterference` (`Synthyrion_NICompK`, on `Synthyrion_ComposeK`). The combined state holds each task row once (inbox, outstanding sends, runnable, held, the notification waited on, the hand-over record) plus the notification objects, and the Synthyrion_InboxK and Synthyrion_NtfnK operations run on views of it and write back, so the flags the kernel shares are shared: a send's wake of its target reads the target's notification-wait state (lib.rs:1836-1850, ipcx.rs:466-475) and a signal's wake of a waiter or bound task writes the same runnable flag a send writes. STRENGTH: UNBOUNDED, MODEL LEVEL, not a refinement. Theorem: under the conjunction of the two component policies over one domain assignment (endpoint WRITE and notification WRITE / READ capabilities each give flow both ways; a queued waiter holds READ; a bound task is one its notification may flow to), from two combined states that agree on every domain that may flow to u, any run of send / receive / signal / wait actions gives u (its full task rows and its notification objects) the same view as only the actions of domains that may flow to u. The proof lifts the two component proofs through the views (the views are faithful: a write-back of an unchanged view is the identity, no row appears or disappears, and each component keeps the flags the other owns). PLANTS, the cross-component leaks the composition exists for, convicted on a concrete combined state (green convict session) with Attempt sessions that FAIL: a signal that also wipes the inbox of a bystander task of another domain, a send that also clears the pending word of a notification of another domain. STILL NOT covered by THIS paragraph (stated; call / reply / fault non-interference is the next paragraph): IRQ delivery and handler operations (integrity only), capability transfer, timing channels, any code-level refinement. **2026-11-10, spec level (call / reply / fault non-interference):** (1) `Synthyrion_NICallK` (628d3e130, a queued submission), over `Synthyrion_CallK` alone: `cstep_inv`, `cstep_local_respect`, `cstep_step_consistency` and the unwinding instance `cn_noninterference` for call, reply, collect, fault, restart and receive; `reply_stranger_noop` (a reply by a task the caller does not trust is the identity) and `oldest_eq` (the choice of the oldest unread fault report is the same in both states). (2) `Synthyrion_ComposeCK` and `Synthyrion_NICompCK` (fb78950f6, a queued submission): ONE combined state holds every task row once (inbox, outstanding sends, runnable, notification wait and hand-over, and the call / reply-slot / fault / handler / restart / sequence fields; the notification objects), with "held" DERIVED (call-waiting, fault-held or notification-waiting) so the flags the kernel shares are one field each; the three components (send / receive, signal / wait, call / reply / collect / fault / restart / receive) run on views and write back; `xstep_inv` (each component keeps the other two components' invariants through shape lemmas), `xstep_local_respect`, `xstep_step_consistency` and `xcomp_noninterference` over all the action kinds: from two combined states that agree on every domain that may flow to u, any run gives u (its full task rows, eleven fields, and its notification objects) the same view as only the actions of domains that may flow to u. POLICY: endpoint WRITE (send and call), notification WRITE / READ and Task WRITE (restart) each give flow both ways; the reply slot, the Calls waiting in an inbox and the fault handler are all authorised with the owner (invariant). NO SETUP ASSUMPTION about fault handlers (the earlier assumption `hndc`, every task has a live fault handler, is REMOVED by `Synthyrion_NIExitCK`, e195156b9, a queued submission): the exit protocol is brought over the whole combined state, and a fault of a task without a live handler IS that exit (the Kill branch). The exit (modelled row by row from `forget_task_ipcx`, ipcx.rs:1367-1405, and `bounce_inbox_of`, Synthyrion_InboxKExit): the dying task's inbox is drained and every word message goes back to its sender as a Bounce (woken unless held, refused when full); the calls it made are withdrawn from the inboxes; every caller blocked on it is aborted (loses its slot, holds the Aborted result, woken); every task registered with it as handler loses the registration; it leaves the notification wait queues and every notification bound to it is unbound; its row disappears. A reply slot of a FAULT owed to the dead handler is left stranded and the faulted task is retired by the same exit action of its own domain: the model has more interleavings than the kernel's atomic cascade, which is the safe direction for non-interference. `xstep2_inv` (the invariant, without `hndc`, is kept by every action including the exit), `xstep2_local_respect` (an exit touches only rows and notification objects the dying task's domain may flow to), `xstep2_step_consistency`, `xexit_noninterference` over the actions above plus the exit of a task. NEW POLICY CONTENT found by this unit: a notification bound to a task must give flow BOTH WAYS between the notification and the task (`bound_back`), because the exit of the bound task drops the binding and so changes the notification object (the notification's domain observes the task's death); `Synthyrion_NINtfnK` had only the edge from the notification to the task. Plants for the exit, convicted on a concrete state with Attempt sessions that FAIL: an exit that aborts every blocked caller (not only those owed by the dying task) and an exit that withdraws every queued Call from every inbox. PLANTS convicted on concrete states (green convict sessions) with Attempt sessions that FAIL: in `Synthyrion_NICallK` a reply without the receiver check, a fault report delivered to a task that is not the handler, a call without the endpoint authority; in the combined state, interferences visible ONLY there, a call that also signals the target's bound notification (it changes a notification object of a domain the caller cannot flow to) and a send that ignores the call-held flag (it wakes a caller blocked on its reply and the combined invariant is lost). Strength: UNBOUNDED, MODEL LEVEL, not a refinement. NOT covered (stated): reply-direction capability transfer and revocation of transferred copies (the send / call transfer and the GRANT right are covered: see the capability-transfer paragraph of F13b), scheduling-context donation, exit-time abort of calls and faults, timeout faults, TCB suspension, IRQ delivery and the IRQ handler operations (integrity only), timing channels, any code-level refinement. **2026-11-10, spec level (capability transfer and GRANT in the combined non-interference state):** `Synthyrion_NICapXK` (e84d4a1e7, a queued submission, session SYNTHYRION_NICapXK on SYNTHYRION_NIIrqK; no sorry; raw log docs/proofs/ISABELLE_BUILD_LOG_2026-11-10_NICAPXK.txt). KERNEL PATH MODELLED (read from the source): at SEND time carrying any capability needs GRANT on the endpoint capability used, at most MAX_XFER_CAPS (3), each carried capability resolved by pointer in the SENDER's own CSpace (`check_xfer` ipcx.rs:688-709, `call_to` ipcx.rs:847-851, rule ipcx.rs:126, resolve cspace.rs:1135-1139); at RECEIVE time `install_xfer` (cspace.rs:1149-1186) puts capability i into slot first+i of the receiver's named receive CNode as a derivation CHILD of the sender's node with the sender's rights; it is dropped (the message still arrives) when the slot is occupied, the source has since been deleted, or the receive CNode is unusable. The transfer is a COPY: the sender keeps its capability. STATE: per task its capability slots, its queue of pending transfers and its node-id counter are added to the observed state of the combined model (all earlier action kinds, the exit and the IRQ actions unchanged). Actions: capability-carrying send/call, receive-and-install, delete of one's own slot. POLICY ANSWER: a GRANT endpoint gives information flow both ways between the two tasks, but that edge ALREADY exists (`ep_flow`: a send reads the target's queue room, a receive's install reads the sender's slot); GRANT adds NO new information-flow edge. It adds AUTHORITY: a one-way authority edge sender to receiver. Under the policy it needs `grant_auth` (the receiver authorised for everything the sender may hold: the kernel lets the sender pick any capability it holds, so the policy can only bound a GRANT endpoint by the sender's whole authority) and `cap_flow` (holding a capability on an object gives flow both ways with that object's domain). Invariants: every capability in a slot is on an object its holder is authorised for, every queued entry has a GRANT endpoint from its sender and an authorised object. `transfer_creates_no_new_flow`: every installed capability lies on an object whose domain the receiver already flows to and from; authority moves only along authorised edges. THEOREM `xcap_noninterference` over every earlier action kind plus the three new ones; also `tsend_needs_grant`, `tsend_keeps_the_sender_capability` (copy, not move), `trecv_never_overwrites`. STRENGTH: UNBOUNDED, MODEL LEVEL, not a refinement. PLANTS convicted on a concrete state (green convict session) with Attempt sessions that FAIL: a transfer without GRANT (changes a domain the sender cannot flow to), a receive that overwrites an occupied slot, a transfer that moves where the kernel copies. MODEL CHOICES (disclosed): the carried list sits in a parallel per-task queue, not inside the inbox entry (a capability-carrying send is not refused here when the word inbox is full); node ids are per task (the kernel's allocator is global and ids are not user visible); exit does not clear slots; the receive CNode validity check is taken as satisfied. NOT covered (stated): the REPLY-direction transfer, REVOCATION of a transferred copy (the copy is a derivation child, so the sender's revoke changes the receiver's CSpace: the sender-to-receiver edge is already in `ep_flow`; ruling; the revoke is modelled in `Synthyrion_CSpaceK`, a different model), the stale-epoch check, scheduling-context creation and the abort / exit return of a donated context (donation over call and its return are covered: see the scheduling-context paragraph of F10; the budget is a timing channel and is not modelled), timing channels, any code-level refinement.

Every piece of evidence below is on main (ee4fd798b, b03b15931, 6319d22f4); the sweeps tests/fastpath.rs and tests/gate_fast.rs were re-run on main plus the reply-slot unit on 2026-10-31…

### Gap or limit, in full

Every piece of evidence below is on main (`ee4fd798b`, `b03b15931`, `6319d22f4`); the sweeps `tests/fastpath.rs` and `tests/gate_fast.rs` were re-run on main plus the reply-slot unit on 2026-10-31 and pass. The fastpath exists at the model level (2026-10-23, `call_fast` / `reply_recv_fast`, `ipcx.rs`): a straight-line route for a word message to a waiting receiver (inbox empty) and for a reply to a live call slot with nothing donated, everything else handed to the slow path; `fast_path_stats` counts taken vs fell back; `tests/fastpath.rs`: a bounded-exhaustive differential sweep (7 operations, depth 6, two worlds (client A on a scheduling context, so its calls donate it), 235,298 sequences, two kernels each; results, server queue, open calls, the scheduler's whole state, both clients and every task's scheduling-context budget equal after every step; asserts the fast path was taken > 10,000, fell back > 10,000 times and ran on a donated context in > 10,000 steps so it cannot pass vacuously), a stats test and a fault-reply test; 8 plants RED, including one that loosens eligibility to admit a donated slot. Arch level (2026-10-25): the ring-3 gate's call (selector 34) and reply (35, and the reply half of 54) now take the same straight-line route in the booted UEFI image: ONE body per route (`call_to` with a `fast` flag, `fast_reply_route`, shared by the model-level `call_fast` / `reply_recv_fast` and the gate; no second copy), everything ineligible handed to `cs_call` / `reply`. `tests/gate_fast.rs`: a bounded-exhaustive differential THROUGH THE GATE (235,298 sequences, two kernels each; the gate's answer, the caller's memory record, queue, open calls, scheduler state and every scheduling-context budget equal at every step; non-vacuity asserts), a round-trip and a refusal test; 12 plants RED. Booted (`gate_uefi_proof.py`, QEMU/TCG): the first call and reply took the route (2 taken, 0 slow), the same call forced onto the slow route gives the same answer, plant `gate_plant_forceslow` turns the route checks RED. STILL TRUE, now stated limits and not a reason for PARTIAL: the route is ordinary Rust inside the kernel plus a hook in the gate, not a hand-written assembly stub that skips the generic trap path; and no silicon cycle number exists: the rdtsc timings of the two routes are QEMU/TCG, cold, INDICATIVE ONLY, so no claim of being faster than the slow path is made. Silicon cycles are the testing phase 2026-11-10 ISABELLE MODEL: 2026-11-10 MODEL PROOF, Isabelle/HOL (no sorry, raw log docs/proofs/ISABELLE_BUILD_LOG_2026-11-10_REPLYRECVK.txt). STRENGTH: UNBOUNDED, MODEL LEVEL, written from the Rust source with file:line in the theory header (reply_recv ipcx.rs:1065-1074, fast_reply_route:2393-2410, reply_recv_fast:2444-2456, over the call / reply model `Synthyrion_CallK`); NOT a refinement of the code and NOT a statement about cycles or the booted route. `Synthyrion_ReplyRecvK` (session SYNTHYRION_InboxK): `reply_recv_atomic_on_a_bad_receive_capability` and `reply_recv_atomic_on_a_refused_reply`: a refusal of EITHER half makes NEITHER happen (the receive capability is checked first; a refused reply never reaches the receive); `reply_recv_is_reply_then_receive`: when the reply succeeds the step is exactly reply then receive; `reply_recv_inv` and `reply_recv_fast_inv`: the call / fault invariant (a slot exists exactly when its owner waits for a reply or is fault-held, never both, blocked tasks are not runnable) is kept by the combined step and by the fast route; `reply_recv_fast_equals_slow`: THE DIFFERENTIAL STATEMENT OF THE MODEL, for every state and every input, the fast route (slot live, issued to the replier, a CALL not a fault report, no donated context) returns the same answer and the same state as the slow path; the model drops the donated scheduling context because the fast route's eligibility requires none and for such a slot the slow path's context calls are no-ops (ipcx.rs:2448-2451). This is the same claim the bounded sweeps `tests/fastpath.rs` and `tests/gate_fast.rs` check on the code, proved for the model on all states instead of 235,298 sequences. PLANTS convicted on concrete states (green convict session) with Attempt sessions that FAIL: a fast route that does not check the slot was issued to the replier (a stranger completes a call), a fast route that also takes a fault report (the report is answered as a call and the task stays suspended), a reply_recv that receives although the reply was refused. NOT covered (stated): the gate route behind capability pointers (`cs_call_fast`, `reply_word_fast`), scheduling-context donation, any timing or cycle claim, and any code-level refinement. 2026-11-10, non-interference: `Synthyrion_NICallK` / `Synthyrion_NICompCK` prove non-interference of the SEPARATE reply and receive (call, reply, collect, fault, restart, receive, with the combined state; 628d3e130 and fb78950f6); the ATOMIC reply_recv step of `Synthyrion_ReplyRecvK` is NOT in that non-interference theorem (it is not an action there); the Kill branch is covered through the exit of `Synthyrion_NIExitCK` (e195156b9). Status word unchanged.

### Gap or limit, in full

Two catalogue rows PARTIAL (no TCB-wait-on-endpoint-and-notification combination) **2026-11-10, ISABELLE MODELS:** 2026-11-10, spec level, Isabelle/HOL (Isabelle2025-2 on the proof VM, no sorry, raw logs docs/proofs/ISABELLE_BUILD_LOG_2026-11-10_NTFNK.txt and _INTEGK.txt). STRENGTH: UNBOUNDED, MODEL LEVEL; written from the Rust source with file:line in the theory header (cs_signal ipcx.rs:1004, raise ipcx.rs:1832-1857, cs_wait ipcx.rs:1893-1920); NOT a refinement: the kernel is not proved to implement the model. `Synthyrion_NtfnK` (session SYNTHYRION_InboxK): a signal ORs its badge into the word (signals do not queue or count: `signal_twice_is_once`; `signal_keeps_bits`: no pending bit is ever lost), a badge of 0, a missing WRITE or a destroyed notification is Denied and an unknown signaller Invalid, the oldest blocked waiter is handed the word and the word is cleared (`signal_hands_word_to_waiter`), the bound task is woken unless it is held, a pending word is returned before the idle check, a held task cannot wait, a waiter joins the tail. Invariant `inv_n` kept by `signal_inv` and `wait_inv`: the waiter queue is exactly the set of waiting tasks, without duplicates, and a queued waiter implies an empty word. `Synthyrion_IntegK`: `signal_integrity` (only the notification, its oldest waiter and its bound task change) and `wait_integrity` (the caller and the notification); refusals change nothing. PLANTS convicted on concrete states (green convict session) with Attempt sessions that FAIL: max-merge instead of OR (the old `Synthyrion_Ipc.signal_op`), a waiter handed 0 instead of the word. NOT covered (stated): NON-INTERFERENCE OVER SIGNAL / WAIT IN THE NEW MODEL WAS NOT PROVED WHEN THIS LINE WAS WRITTEN (it is now: see the correction at the end of this cell) (it needs the waiter and bound-task authority edges in the policy; only integrity is); scheduling-context lending, the run queue, the sealed API, and any code-level refinement. The OLDER Isabelle signal / wait theorems and the signal / wait cases of `kernel_noninterference2` model a max-merge with no bound wake and still do NOT count for this row. **2026-11-10 CORRECTION:** 2026-11-10 UPDATE, model level, Isabelle/HOL (no sorry, raw log docs/proofs/ISABELLE_BUILD_LOG_2026-11-10_NINTFNK.txt): NON-INTERFERENCE OVER SIGNAL AND WAIT IS NOW PROVED in the notification model, `Synthyrion_NINtfnK` (`nin_noninterference`), strength UNBOUNDED, MODEL LEVEL, not a refinement. Policy: tasks and notification objects carry security domains under a reflexive transitive flow relation; a WRITE capability on a notification (ipcx.rs:1004) and a READ capability (ipcx.rs:1893) each give flow BOTH WAYS between the task and the notification; two state invariants: a task is queued on a notification only if it holds READ on it, and the task a notification is bound to is one it may flow to (cs_bind_notification, ipcx.rs:1928). Theorem: from two states that agree on every domain that may flow to u (and satisfy the invariants), any list of signal / wait actions gives u the same view as only the actions of domains that may flow to u (the unwinding argument is the own copy in `Synthyrion_NIK` with step consistency over the actor's sources). PLANTS convicted on concrete states with Attempt sessions that FAIL: a signal that also wakes a bystander task, a wait that also clears the pending word of a notification of another domain. STILL NOT covered by THIS paragraph (stated; call / reply / fault non-interference is the next paragraph): IRQ delivery and the IRQ handler operations (integrity only), the interaction of notifications with send / recv in one system (the two models have separate state; the shared held flag is not modelled together), scheduling effects, timing channels, any code-level refinement. **2026-11-10 COMPOSITION:** 2026-11-10 COMPOSITION, model level, Isabelle/HOL (no sorry, raw log docs/proofs/ISABELLE_BUILD_LOG_2026-11-10_NICOMPK.txt): the send / receive proof (`Synthyrion_NIK`) and the signal / wait proof (`Synthyrion_NINtfnK`) are now ONE theorem over ONE state: `ucomp_noninterference` (`Synthyrion_NICompK`, on `Synthyrion_ComposeK`). The combined state holds each task row once (inbox, outstanding sends, runnable, held, the notification waited on, the hand-over record) plus the notification objects, and the Synthyrion_InboxK and Synthyrion_NtfnK operations run on views of it and write back, so the flags the kernel shares are shared: a send's wake of its target reads the target's notification-wait state (lib.rs:1836-1850, ipcx.rs:466-475) and a signal's wake of a waiter or bound task writes the same runnable flag a send writes. STRENGTH: UNBOUNDED, MODEL LEVEL, not a refinement. Theorem: under the conjunction of the two component policies over one domain assignment (endpoint WRITE and notification WRITE / READ capabilities each give flow both ways; a queued waiter holds READ; a bound task is one its notification may flow to), from two combined states that agree on every domain that may flow to u, any run of send / receive / signal / wait actions gives u (its full task rows and its notification objects) the same view as only the actions of domains that may flow to u. The proof lifts the two component proofs through the views (the views are faithful: a write-back of an unchanged view is the identity, no row appears or disappears, and each component keeps the flags the other owns). PLANTS, the cross-component leaks the composition exists for, convicted on a concrete combined state (green convict session) with Attempt sessions that FAIL: a signal that also wipes the inbox of a bystander task of another domain, a send that also clears the pending word of a notification of another domain. STILL NOT covered (stated): IRQ delivery and handler operations (integrity only), capability transfer, timing channels, any code-level refinement. **2026-11-10, spec level (call / reply / fault non-interference):** (1) `Synthyrion_NICallK` (628d3e130, a queued submission), over `Synthyrion_CallK` alone: `cstep_inv`, `cstep_local_respect`, `cstep_step_consistency` and the unwinding instance `cn_noninterference` for call, reply, collect, fault, restart and receive; `reply_stranger_noop` (a reply by a task the caller does not trust is the identity) and `oldest_eq` (the choice of the oldest unread fault report is the same in both states). (2) `Synthyrion_ComposeCK` and `Synthyrion_NICompCK` (fb78950f6, a queued submission): ONE combined state holds every task row once (inbox, outstanding sends, runnable, notification wait and hand-over, and the call / reply-slot / fault / handler / restart / sequence fields; the notification objects), with "held" DERIVED (call-waiting, fault-held or notification-waiting) so the flags the kernel shares are one field each; the three components (send / receive, signal / wait, call / reply / collect / fault / restart / receive) run on views and write back; `xstep_inv` (each component keeps the other two components' invariants through shape lemmas), `xstep_local_respect`, `xstep_step_consistency` and `xcomp_noninterference` over all the action kinds: from two combined states that agree on every domain that may flow to u, any run gives u (its full task rows, eleven fields, and its notification objects) the same view as only the actions of domains that may flow to u. POLICY: endpoint WRITE (send and call), notification WRITE / READ and Task WRITE (restart) each give flow both ways; the reply slot, the Calls waiting in an inbox and the fault handler are all authorised with the owner (invariant). NO SETUP ASSUMPTION about fault handlers (the earlier assumption `hndc`, every task has a live fault handler, is REMOVED by `Synthyrion_NIExitCK`, e195156b9, a queued submission): the exit protocol is brought over the whole combined state, and a fault of a task without a live handler IS that exit (the Kill branch). The exit (modelled row by row from `forget_task_ipcx`, ipcx.rs:1367-1405, and `bounce_inbox_of`, Synthyrion_InboxKExit): the dying task's inbox is drained and every word message goes back to its sender as a Bounce (woken unless held, refused when full); the calls it made are withdrawn from the inboxes; every caller blocked on it is aborted (loses its slot, holds the Aborted result, woken); every task registered with it as handler loses the registration; it leaves the notification wait queues and every notification bound to it is unbound; its row disappears. A reply slot of a FAULT owed to the dead handler is left stranded and the faulted task is retired by the same exit action of its own domain: the model has more interleavings than the kernel's atomic cascade, which is the safe direction for non-interference. `xstep2_inv` (the invariant, without `hndc`, is kept by every action including the exit), `xstep2_local_respect` (an exit touches only rows and notification objects the dying task's domain may flow to), `xstep2_step_consistency`, `xexit_noninterference` over the actions above plus the exit of a task. NEW POLICY CONTENT found by this unit: a notification bound to a task must give flow BOTH WAYS between the notification and the task (`bound_back`), because the exit of the bound task drops the binding and so changes the notification object (the notification's domain observes the task's death); `Synthyrion_NINtfnK` had only the edge from the notification to the task. Plants for the exit, convicted on a concrete state with Attempt sessions that FAIL: an exit that aborts every blocked caller (not only those owed by the dying task) and an exit that withdraws every queued Call from every inbox. PLANTS convicted on concrete states (green convict sessions) with Attempt sessions that FAIL: in `Synthyrion_NICallK` a reply without the receiver check, a fault report delivered to a task that is not the handler, a call without the endpoint authority; in the combined state, interferences visible ONLY there, a call that also signals the target's bound notification (it changes a notification object of a domain the caller cannot flow to) and a send that ignores the call-held flag (it wakes a caller blocked on its reply and the combined invariant is lost). Strength: UNBOUNDED, MODEL LEVEL, not a refinement. NOT covered (stated): capability transfer and the GRANT right, scheduling-context donation, exit-time abort of calls and faults, timeout faults, TCB suspension, timing channels, any code-level refinement; IRQ delivery and the IRQ handler operations are covered by the next paragraph. **2026-11-10, spec level (IRQ in the combined non-interference state):** `Synthyrion_NIIrqK` (bb85fdbed, a queued submission, session SYNTHYRION_NIIrqK on SYNTHYRION_NIExitCK; no sorry; raw log docs/proofs/ISABELLE_BUILD_LOG_2026-11-10_NIIRQK.txt): the IRQ handler lifecycle of `Synthyrion_IrqK` (irqctl.rs irq_issue:139, irq_set:173, irq_ack:189, irq_clear:202, irq_revoke:219, irq_fired_handler:279-292) is a FOURTH component of the combined state, a view of the one state with a write-back: a delivery raises the SAME notification objects and wakes the same waiter and bound-task rows a signal wakes. Actions: IRQControl get, bind (set notification), unbind (clear), ack (unmask), REVOKE (drops the binding) and the delivery, besides every earlier action kind and the exit. POLICY (owner rulings and): each hardware line has ONE domain, an input of the policy; binding a line to a notification gives flow BOTH WAYS between the line's domain and the notification's domain and that is a state invariant (every live binding is two-way); a delivery is attributed to the line's domain, get and revoke to the IRQ control domain, which must flow to every line's domain (the line table and the ONE generation counter next_gen are the control domain's state: a handler operation reads the table to check its capability's generation, get and revoke write it); the coalesced count is in no domain's view (nothing reads it: `irq_coalesced` is called only from tests). THEOREM `xirq_noninterference`: from two combined states that agree on every domain that may flow to u, any run of all action kinds (send, receive, signal, wait, call, reply, collect, fault, restart, receive-call, exit, and the six IRQ actions) gives u (its task rows, its notification objects, its lines' binding / mask / legacy flag, and for the control domain the line table and the counter) the same view as only the actions of domains that may flow to u. Lemmas: `qstep_qinv` (the IRQ invariant and the two-way binding are kept by every IRQ action), `masked_lines_are_bound`, `bind_needs_a_live_notification`, `revoke_drops_the_binding` (the line is unbound and unmasked and every capability of the old generation is dead), local respect, step consistency, the unwinding instance. STRENGTH: UNBOUNDED, MODEL LEVEL, not a refinement: the Rust kernel is not proved to implement the model. NO NEW SETUP ASSUMPTION. Disclosed: an unauthorised bind (no capability pair) is the identity, as an unauthorised send, signal or restart is in the other components; return codes are not part of the observed state; the hardware (the line firing, the controller's mask register) is outside the model; the destination-slot refusals of cs_irq_handler_get only add refusals and are not modelled. CONSEQUENCES FOR POLICY CONFIGURATION: a binding between two domains is a two-way channel and must be authorised as such; the IRQ control domain must be able to flow to every line's domain, because get and revoke act on the global line table and counter. PLANTS convicted on a concrete combined state (green convict session) with Attempt sessions that FAIL: a delivery that also wakes a task bound to a DIFFERENT notification (an interference visible only in the combined state), a revoke that leaves the binding live (the revoked line still delivers), a bind with no authority check. NOT covered (stated): reply-direction capability transfer and revocation of transferred copies (the send / call transfer and the GRANT right are covered: see the capability-transfer paragraph of F13b), scheduling-context creation and the abort / exit return of a donated context (donation over call and its return are covered: see the scheduling-context paragraph of F10; the budget is a timing channel and is not modelled), timeout faults, TCB suspension, the run queue, timing channels, any code-level refinement.

### Gap or limit, in full

**2026-10-31 (ROWS work stream, commit `1d6822eac`, on main):** a CPU exception taken at CPL 3 in the default UEFI image (no scene feature) is offered to `synthyrion_hal::set_user_fault_hook`; a delivered fault ends the task and returns to the boot context like an EXIT, and the kernel raises it at the task's fault endpoint (`boot_uefi/src/fault_scene.rs`: task A's fault endpoint is task B's; an #UD task and a #PF task run, the message (task, kind, address, ip) is read back, the supervisor restarts them). Proof `proofs/f17_default_uefi_proof.py`: normal 20/20, plant `f17_plant_nodeliver` (the hook stops the task but never raises the fault) convicted 6/6. RE-RUN 2026-10-31 by the ROWS work stream on main `3fec2ba3a` (QEMU TCG, VM): `f17_default_uefi_proof.py` normal 20/20, plant nodeliver 6/6. Fault kinds the kernel raises: page read/write/exec, unknown syscall, undefined instruction, any other CPU exception (vector in `addr`), VM exit of a bound VCPU, and (F10, `5ef41cffc`, host) a TIMEOUT fault. a failed capability lookup in a gate call answers a refusal status and does not raise a CapFault message to a handler; the fault of a task that has no fault handler registered kills it (`FaultDisposition::Killed`). **Earlier text (kept):** **2026-10-25:** `set_fault_handler` (44), `clear_fault_handler` (45) and `restart_task` (46) are Wired kernel-gate rows, called from ring 3 on the booted image with their refusals (F28a; the fault itself is made to happen by the kernel side in that scene, so the page-fault-to-handler path from a ring-3 task is still the driver-domain host's). Wired in the ring-3 driver-domain host (feature `domain_ipc`) and the AArch64 EL0 scenario, not for arbitrary tasks in the default UEFI image (B11 remainder). Was: only the **B11 addition (2026-10-20/21):** `set_fault_handler` `ipcx.rs`, `raise_fault`, `restart_task`; catalogue PAGEFAULT1001-5 HAVE, 0005 PARTIAL; ON THE UEFI PATH (B11, 2026-10-20): a fault of a driver domain is routed by `boot_uefi/src/domipc.rs` `on_fault` to `raise_fault`, queued at a supervisor task, and the domain is restarted ONLY after the supervisor decides (`hold_restart`); QEMU proofs `dom_bcm5720_uefi_proof.py ipc_fault` 10/10 (decision strictly before the restart, two rounds) with the plant `ipc_nosup` (supervisor decides nothing: never restarted), `dom_ipc_proof.py --uefi fault` for the USB domain || status there: HAVE (QEMU; the x86-64 test server silicon run of the fault path is part of the B11 window, pending) || The corrected row: the earlier text "not in boot_uefi" was true before `domipc.rs` landed (3b49335df). A fault inside the KERNEL (CPL 0) is still a halt, not a message **2026-11-10, ISABELLE MODEL:** 2026-11-10, spec level, Isabelle/HOL (Isabelle2025-2 on the proof VM, no sorry, raw log docs/proofs/ISABELLE_BUILD_LOG_2026-11-10_CALLK.txt). STRENGTH: UNBOUNDED, MODEL LEVEL, written from the Rust source with file:line in the theory header (raise_fault ipcx.rs:2258, report_fault_to:2272, restart_task:2308, reply:1025, receive:1087, call_to:832); NOT a refinement of the code, and it is not a statement about the booted image (those are the QEMU proofs above). `Synthyrion_CallK` (session SYNTHYRION_InboxK): a fault of a held task is NotRunning; a task with no live handler is KILLED; otherwise the task is suspended and not runnable and a fault reply slot (receiver = the handler) is filled WITHOUT using the handler's inbox (`fault_report_leaves_the_handler_inbox_alone`: a full inbox cannot make another task's fault fail); the handler's receive serves the oldest unread fault report BEFORE its inbox and marks it read (`receive_serves_a_fault_first`); a reply by the slot's receiver resumes the faulted task (`reply_resumes_the_faulted_task`), a reply by anyone else is Denied and a stale id Invalid, and a reply is ONE-SHOT (`reply_is_one_shot`); a restart (Task WRITE) of a fault-suspended task drops the fault slot, ends the suspension and counts it (`restart_ends_the_suspension`), and is Invalid for a task that is not fault-suspended. Invariant `inv_c` kept by call, reply, restart, fault, receive and collect (`call_inv`, `reply_inv`, `restart_inv`, `fault_inv`, `receive_inv`, `collect_inv`): a slot exists exactly when its owner waits for a reply or is fault-held, never both, and a blocked task is not runnable and not waiting on a notification. Integrity per operation (`call_integrity`, `reply_integrity`, `fault_integrity`, `restart_integrity`): a reply changes only the caller's row, a restart only the restarted task's. The same file proves the CALL side: a call needs an idle caller, queues one Call at the target tail, blocks the caller, and a full inbox blocks nothing (`call_full_inbox_blocks_nothing`); `reply_completes_the_call`. PLANTS convicted on concrete states (green convict session) with Attempt sessions that FAIL: a reply that does not consume its slot, a reply accepted from a stranger, a restart that leaves the fault slot behind. NOT covered by the model above (stated; non-interference is the next paragraph): capability transfer and the GRANT right; scheduling-context donation; the exit-time abort of calls and faults (`forget_task_ipcx`); timeout faults; TCB suspension; the CapFault message (a DIFFERENT DESIGN already named in this row); any code-level refinement. **2026-11-10, spec level (call / reply / fault non-interference):** (1) `Synthyrion_NICallK` (628d3e130, a queued submission), over `Synthyrion_CallK` alone: `cstep_inv`, `cstep_local_respect`, `cstep_step_consistency` and the unwinding instance `cn_noninterference` for call, reply, collect, fault, restart and receive; `reply_stranger_noop` (a reply by a task the caller does not trust is the identity) and `oldest_eq` (the choice of the oldest unread fault report is the same in both states). (2) `Synthyrion_ComposeCK` and `Synthyrion_NICompCK` (fb78950f6, a queued submission): ONE combined state holds every task row once (inbox, outstanding sends, runnable, notification wait and hand-over, and the call / reply-slot / fault / handler / restart / sequence fields; the notification objects), with "held" DERIVED (call-waiting, fault-held or notification-waiting) so the flags the kernel shares are one field each; the three components (send / receive, signal / wait, call / reply / collect / fault / restart / receive) run on views and write back; `xstep_inv` (each component keeps the other two components' invariants through shape lemmas), `xstep_local_respect`, `xstep_step_consistency` and `xcomp_noninterference` over all the action kinds: from two combined states that agree on every domain that may flow to u, any run gives u (its full task rows, eleven fields, and its notification objects) the same view as only the actions of domains that may flow to u. POLICY: endpoint WRITE (send and call), notification WRITE / READ and Task WRITE (restart) each give flow both ways; the reply slot, the Calls waiting in an inbox and the fault handler are all authorised with the owner (invariant). NO SETUP ASSUMPTION about fault handlers (the earlier assumption `hndc`, every task has a live fault handler, is REMOVED by `Synthyrion_NIExitCK`, e195156b9, a queued submission): the exit protocol is brought over the whole combined state, and a fault of a task without a live handler IS that exit (the Kill branch). The exit (modelled row by row from `forget_task_ipcx`, ipcx.rs:1367-1405, and `bounce_inbox_of`, Synthyrion_InboxKExit): the dying task's inbox is drained and every word message goes back to its sender as a Bounce (woken unless held, refused when full); the calls it made are withdrawn from the inboxes; every caller blocked on it is aborted (loses its slot, holds the Aborted result, woken); every task registered with it as handler loses the registration; it leaves the notification wait queues and every notification bound to it is unbound; its row disappears. A reply slot of a FAULT owed to the dead handler is left stranded and the faulted task is retired by the same exit action of its own domain: the model has more interleavings than the kernel's atomic cascade, which is the safe direction for non-interference. `xstep2_inv` (the invariant, without `hndc`, is kept by every action including the exit), `xstep2_local_respect` (an exit touches only rows and notification objects the dying task's domain may flow to), `xstep2_step_consistency`, `xexit_noninterference` over the actions above plus the exit of a task. NEW POLICY CONTENT found by this unit: a notification bound to a task must give flow BOTH WAYS between the notification and the task (`bound_back`), because the exit of the bound task drops the binding and so changes the notification object (the notification's domain observes the task's death); `Synthyrion_NINtfnK` had only the edge from the notification to the task. Plants for the exit, convicted on a concrete state with Attempt sessions that FAIL: an exit that aborts every blocked caller (not only those owed by the dying task) and an exit that withdraws every queued Call from every inbox. PLANTS convicted on concrete states (green convict sessions) with Attempt sessions that FAIL: in `Synthyrion_NICallK` a reply without the receiver check, a fault report delivered to a task that is not the handler, a call without the endpoint authority; in the combined state, interferences visible ONLY there, a call that also signals the target's bound notification (it changes a notification object of a domain the caller cannot flow to) and a send that ignores the call-held flag (it wakes a caller blocked on its reply and the combined invariant is lost). Strength: UNBOUNDED, MODEL LEVEL, not a refinement. NOT covered (stated): reply-direction capability transfer and revocation of transferred copies (the send / call transfer and the GRANT right are covered: see the capability-transfer paragraph of F13b), scheduling-context creation and the abort / exit return of a donated context (donation over call and its return are covered: see the scheduling-context paragraph of F10; the budget is a timing channel and is not modelled), exit-time abort of calls and faults, timeout faults, TCB suspension, IRQ delivery and the IRQ handler operations (integrity only), timing channels, any code-level refinement.

**HAVE FOR THE x86 UEFI PATH ONLY: `Kernel::irq_fired(line)` (a bare line number) is still a public bypass of the handler capability, and the AArch32 and RISC-V live hosts and the AArch64 live host still call it, so on those images a handler is not yet the only way in. F18 ENFORCEMENT CLOSED ON THE x86 UEFI PATH.** Every hardware interrupt the UEFI image delivers now goes THROUGH the line's IRQHandler capability: `Kernel::irq_fired_via(&handler)` (`irqctl.rs`) signals only for a handler that verifies (`NotAHandler` otherwise), whose generation is the line's current one (`Stale` once revoked, also when a NEW handler owns and is bound on the line), whose line is bound (`Unbound`) and unmasked (`Masked` until the ack); `boot_uefi/src/domipc.rs` `deliver_line` is the ONLY delivery in that host (the polled INTx sample and the MSI vector path both end there; a line with no handler never asks the kernel) and it prints the refusal reason (`why=`). a kernel that never calls `seal_irq_minting` keeps the legacy path; (3) interrupts are still taken in a window between slices (IF=0), INTx-only devices use the polled sample, MSI-X and more than one core are B11 remainders; SILICON FINDING (PRG 5718-PG107-R p.470): the BCM5720 powers up with One-Shot MSI mode enabled (0x6000 bit 5 clear): it sends ONE MSI and auto-masks; a driver that does not use Tagged Status must set bit 5. The plant `irq_noeoi` on silicon: see the next evidence file **2026-11-04, spec level:** `irq_binding_needs_handler_cap`, `irq_mask_needs_handler_cap`, `irq_bind_needs_signal_cap`, `irq_delivery_needs_handler_and_binding`, `signals_only_by_delivery`, `hinv_reach`, `hw_no_amplification` (`Spec/HwAuth.lean`): only a holder of the live handler capability (Device, READ+WRITE) of a line can change that line's binding or mask; a bind needs a Notification capability with WRITE and an unbound line; a signal is raised only through a handler, for a bound and unmasked line, to the bound notification, and masks the line; no hardware step creates or widens a capability. Strength per theorem: UNBOUNDED, SPEC LEVEL: Lean 4 (`proofs/lean/Synthyrion/Spec/`, unit proof_mem_hw 61800), every state of the abstract specification and every execution length, axiom scan clean (no non-standard axiom); NOT a code-level refinement: the Rust kernel is not proved to refine the specification (`docs/proofs/LEAN_PROGRESS.md`, 2026-11-04). NOT modelled: the issue and revoke of handlers, ranges and spaces (the control authorities). **2026-11-10, spec level (IRQ in the combined non-interference state):** `Synthyrion_NIIrqK` (bb85fdbed, a queued submission, session SYNTHYRION_NIIrqK on SYNTHYRION_NIExitCK; no sorry; raw log docs/proofs/ISABELLE_BUILD_LOG_2026-11-10_NIIRQK.txt): the IRQ handler lifecycle of `Synthyrion_IrqK` (irqctl.rs irq_issue:139, irq_set:173, irq_ack:189, irq_clear:202, irq_revoke:219, irq_fired_handler:279-292) is a FOURTH component of the combined state, a view of the one state with a write-back: a delivery raises the SAME notification objects and wakes the same waiter and bound-task rows a signal wakes. Actions: IRQControl get, bind (set notification), unbind (clear), ack (unmask), REVOKE (drops the binding) and the delivery, besides every earlier action kind and the exit. POLICY (owner rulings and): each hardware line has ONE domain, an input of the policy; binding a line to a notification gives flow BOTH WAYS between the line's domain and the notification's domain and that is a state invariant (every live binding is two-way); a delivery is attributed to the line's domain, get and revoke to the IRQ control domain, which must flow to every line's domain (the line table and the ONE generation counter next_gen are the control domain's state: a handler operation reads the table to check its capability's generation, get and revoke write it); the coalesced count is in no domain's view (nothing reads it: `irq_coalesced` is called only from tests). THEOREM `xirq_noninterference`: from two combined states that agree on every domain that may flow to u, any run of all action kinds (send, receive, signal, wait, call, reply, collect, fault, restart, receive-call, exit, and the six IRQ actions) gives u (its task rows, its notification objects, its lines' binding / mask / legacy flag, and for the control domain the line table and the counter) the same view as only the actions of domains that may flow to u. Lemmas: `qstep_qinv` (the IRQ invariant and the two-way binding are kept by every IRQ action), `masked_lines_are_bound`, `bind_needs_a_live_notification`, `revoke_drops_the_binding` (the line is unbound and unmasked and every capability of the old generation is dead), local respect, step consistency, the unwinding instance. STRENGTH: UNBOUNDED, MODEL LEVEL, not a refinement: the Rust kernel is not proved to implement the model. NO NEW SETUP ASSUMPTION. Disclosed: an unauthorised bind (no capability pair) is the identity, as an unauthorised send, signal or restart is in the other components; return codes are not part of the observed state; the hardware (the line firing, the controller's mask register) is outside the model; the destination-slot refusals of cs_irq_handler_get only add refusals and are not modelled. CONSEQUENCES FOR POLICY CONFIGURATION: a binding between two domains is a two-way channel and must be authorised as such; the IRQ control domain must be able to flow to every line's domain, because get and revoke act on the global line table and counter. PLANTS convicted on a concrete combined state (green convict session) with Attempt sessions that FAIL: a delivery that also wakes a task bound to a DIFFERENT notification (an interference visible only in the combined state), a revoke that leaves the binding live (the revoked line still delivers), a bind with no authority check. NOT covered (stated): reply-direction capability transfer and revocation of transferred copies (the send / call transfer and the GRANT right are covered: see the capability-transfer paragraph of F13b), scheduling-context creation and the abort / exit return of a donated context (donation over call and its return are covered: see the scheduling-context paragraph of F10; the budget is a timing channel and is not modelled), timeout faults, TCB suspension, the run queue, timing channels, any code-level refinement.

Scene `boot_aarch64/src/cache_scene.rs` (EL1 and EL2): one `DC CIVAC` per hardware line for a whole frame and for a partial range (counts computed independently of the walk), data intact, a clean keeps the data, and (EL1) two instructions written as data, unified, the page made read-only/executable, called, returning 42; plant `cache_walk_short` (the walk stops one line early) RED at both exception levels (evidence `boot_aarch64/evidence/f21_g2_2026-10-31/`). **Honest strength of G2:** QEMU TCG implements no data or instruction cache and executes cache-maintenance as a no-op (measured: a `DC CIVAC` at an unmapped address does NOT fault there), so the scene is INSTRUCTION-LEVEL strength and the ordering and coverage proofs are HOST-model strength; whether a line left a cache is not measured, and a stale instruction fetch cannot be reproduced under TCG.
