It is a model, not a refinement of `synthyrion_vmm`. FINDING recorded by the proof work stream and CORRECTED in unit proof_mem_hw 72400: the Lean spec's `Step.map` overwrote an already-mapped page, unlike the kernel; `Step.map` now refuses a mapped page, matching the kernel's Denied (the object path's Occupied), theorem `map_refuses_mapped_page`, plant ST1 (which restores the overwrite) convicted; `VStep.map` requires the page unmapped and `step_map_is_vmap` ties the two.

**2026-10-24 (F22b + B25): EPT AND NPT are capability objects with the VSpace retype / revoke discipline** -- `retype_ept_on(ut, hv, backend)` (F6/F7-f's `retype_ept` for a named family: one frame, empty, Intel EPT or AMD NPT), `ept_map` / `ept_unmap` by capability from frames the holder owns (MAP on the space, MAP + READ / WRITE on the frame, never more than the mapper held), `ept_destroy`; an NPT space refuses the entries AMD cannot express (execute-only, write-only), a VCPU binds only to a space of its own backend, revoking the untyped destroys the space, its mappings and the VCPUs under it; host `tests/ept_retype.rs` 8, 3 plants RED (the space dying with its region is F6/F7-f's); **VM: the AMD scene now runs its guest under a retyped NPT object in QEMU TCG (`RVEUMNZ`, execute-only refused)**. The hardware tables are built from the SAME model (`hypervisor::stage2`: VMSAv8-64, 4 KiB granule, 39-bit IPA, start level 1, `VTCR_EL2` / `VTTBR_EL2`, S2AP / XN / AF / MemAttr leaves; the table walk agrees with `Ept::access` on every probed access) and an EL2 abort (EC 0x24 / 0x20, DFSC / IFSC, `HPFAR_EL2`) reaches the VMM as the same EPT-violation fault message (`vcpu_exited_arm`). VM (QEMU TCG `virt,virtualization=on`, cortex-a72, GICv2, EL2): `boot_aarch64/src/vm_scene.rs`, an EL1 guest under the kernel at EL2: RW page round trip, RO page read, a WRITE to the RO page takes a stage-2 permission fault (the VMM refuses, the frame is unchanged), an unmapped page takes a translation fault that the VMM answers with `ept_map` and the same store is retried and reads back; revoking the untyped region destroys the space, its mappings and the VCPU; 12 ` vm-*` checks PASS, skipped by name at EL1, 6 image plants each RED by the named check (`f22_vm_proof.py`, evidence `boot_aarch64/evidence/f22_arm_2026-11-02/`). VM scene (`vm_scene.rs::run_two`): TWO guests, each in its own stage-2 space and VMID (VTTBR_EL2 switched at every yield), alternate on ONE core for 3 rounds; each writes values of its own into 8 of those registers, yields, and on return checks them all (mask 0 = intact), and the saved state of each holds its own last values; 3 ` vm2-*` checks PASS, 2 image plants RED by name (`f22_vm_proof.py --scenario el1_skip_tpidr|el1_skip_vbar_restore`). The walk model reads blocks, so it still agrees with the kernel's model on every probed access. **2026-10-24 (F22c): a guest takes an external interrupt, an exception and an NMI, each injected BY CAPABILITY** -- `vcpu_inject` (vector, pending set, window when IF=0 or shadow) and `vcpu_inject_event` (NMI / exception / software interrupt, one pending slot, validated at the call against what both backends refuse at entry, WRITE-checked), delivered by the kernel's rule at `vcpu_enter` as `Inject::Event`; host tests `vcpu_inject.rs` 7 + `hypervisor::apply` 3 new, 6 plants RED; **VM evidence: `inj_scene` on AMD-V in QEMU TCG, the guest printed `RVEUMNZ` (V through a VINTR window, U = #UD, N = NMI), three omitted-injection plants each FAIL by the missing letter**. Not here: Intel VMX path: injection and the NMI window run at VM strength under nested VT-x (see INTEL below; the 2026-10-24 wording "structural only" is superseded), bare metal on the x86-64 test server not run (B23), APICv / AVIC not enabled, **2026-11-03 (work stream F26, F22c NMI window; HOST + VM (QEMU TCG AMD-V), not silicon): a held NMI now asks for the exit that fires when NMI blocking ENDS** -- the kernel tracks NMI blocking itself on AMD (an injected NMI blocks until an `iret` completes; AMD does not report it, and before this a second NMI requested inside a handler was injected INTO it), Intel NMI-window exiting (control bit 22, exit 8; refused unless pin-based NMI exiting + virtual NMIs are on) and AMD's standing IRET intercept (74h) followed by ONE single-stepped instruction (TF + #DB intercept, 41h) so the `iret` has completed before the held NMI goes in. Host: `tests/vcpu_nmiwin.rs` 7 tests + `hypervisor::apply` 3 new, six plants RED by name (`plant_hv_nmi_nowindow`, `plant_hv_nmi_noblocktrack`, `plant_hv_nmi_unblock_at_iret`, `plant_hv_nmi_nostep`, `plant_apply_nmi_window_dropped`, `plant_apply_iret_intercept_dropped`). VM: the `inj_scene` NMI phase, the guest printed `RVEUMNnNnZ` (two NMI handlers strictly one after the other) through 2 IRET intercepts and 2 single-step traps; scene plants `svm_plant_nmi_noblocktrack` (nested, `RVEUMNNnnZ`), `..._unblock_at_iret`, `..._nostep`, `svm_plant_iret_intercept_dropped` each FAIL it (evidence `boot_uefi/hwrun/inj_nmi_window_runs_20261103`). STATED LIMITS: a guest with TF already set at its `iret` is released AT the `iret` (the only way an NMI can nest, pinned by a test); `plant_hv_nmi_nowindow` is convicted by the host test, NOT by the scene (on AMD the IRET watch is standing); INTEL, added 2026-11-04 from the landed evidence of unit 44400 (VM strength: Intel VT-x under nested KVM, Hyper-V -> WSL KVM -> Synthyrion, NOT silicon): `vmx_inj_scene` ran an Intel guest through the NMI window and printed `RVEUMNnNnZHW` (`nmi_window_exits=1`, 4 injections accepted and 0 refused), and a host NMI raised from outside while the guest spun reached the HOST handler (`host_nmi vm_exits=1 reached_host_handler=1`); three plants are convicted by the scene (`nmi_nowindow`, `nmi_window_dropped`, `host_nmi_swallowed`); evidence `boot_uefi/hwrun/vmhost_wsl_evidence_20261104T081935Z_vmx_inj_scene.txt` (normal) and its `_PLANT_` siblings; the nested L1 reports no APIC-virtualisation feature, so APICv is not exercised; Intel silicon not run; AMD silicon not run. ### Gap or limit, in full

SMP is in the model at stated bounds (units 1-3) and the per-core scheduler, x2APIC, ids above 255, exceptions / IST / guard pages are shown in QEMU (a VM; see 1c for bounds); the big lock is model-checked as implemented (the real ticket-lock methods over every interleaving, 5 domains, 1,339,284 states) and the TLB shootdown is model-checked and RUNS the same sequence in QEMU at 2-16 cores (units 1-4 of 1c, 2026-10-24), both at stated bounds, not proven for all sizes; x86 SMP silicon (x86-64 test server, 2026-10-25): the TLB shootdown scene PASSED on S3_tlb and its S4_tlb_nopost control was RED as required; the S5_tlb_irqoff control PASSED when it must fail, so the silicon claim covers S3 and S4 ONLY (cause found: the scene could not overlap a lock wait with a pending shootdown on fast silicon; fixed in the scene, QEMU re-verified, silicon NOT re-run); ARM only under QEMU TCG (B15). **F23 U5 (2026-10-29): the MICROKERNEL's TLB invalidations reach every core** (`microkernel/src/tlbcores.rs`, `15d19410b`): `smp_tlb_enable`, `space_switch_on` records per-core PCID residency, `tlb_shootdown_begin` names exactly the cores that may cache a queued entry (plus cores to evict from a disposed space), frames freed by a call are HELD until `tlb_shootdown_complete` and re-scrubbed, owner-changed PCIDs are flushed on every core, a loaded space is invalidated after a PCID wrap; host model of N per-core TLBs (`tests/f23_kernel_shootdown.rs`, 9 tests; STALE / REUSE / LEAK checked after every call), 8 plants RED (HOST). VM scene (`boot_uefi/src/smp_tlbk.rs`, feature `smp_tlbk`, phase 93, `40cb60e40`): the kernel's `frame_unmap` / `map_frame` change the mapping, its plan drives the IPIs; QEMU TCG `-smp 4`: `tlbk` 17/17 and `tlbk_x2` 19/19 (48 rounds, resident cores 0x7, kernel targets 0x6, the outsider core never named and never interrupted, 0 stale reads, 0 reads of a freed frame); plants `tlbk_targets_none` and `tlbk_unrecorded` both FAIL the line with real stale reads and reads of a freed frame. ### Gap or limit, in full

`boot_root_task` also ARMS domain isolation (`180def6d6`: `BootError::Unprotected` refuses an unprotected start, 2 tests, plant `plant_boot_unarmed`). **F24 in the UEFI image (2026-10-21; `boot_uefi/src/main.rs` `live_kernel` / `live_boot_root_task`; proof `proofs/bootarm_uefi_proof.py`, QEMU TCG/OVMF, VM not bare metal):** the live ring-3 kernel the image's syscalls run against is built by `Kernel::boot_root_task`. The root task (the sender of the ring-3 demo, slot 1 of its own CNode) is the only task the boot admits; its root CNode holds its Task capability, the CNode, the schedule and spawn authorities, the Untyped capabilities and two Device capabilities (COM1 `0x3F8` read/write, i8042 `0x60` read); the second task is retyped out of the root's first untyped and admitted with the spawn authority, then object creation is sealed. Read back in the image (`BOOTROOT` lines): normal 22/22, plants `bootroot_plant_selftest` (6/6), `bootmem_plant_overlap_ok` (2/2) and `bootmem_plant_model` (2/2) convicted. **Part 2a:** the live kernel's memory is the firmware map's CONVENTIONAL regions (the two largest at or above 1 MiB, at most 64 MiB each) with the loader's ranges RESERVED, and the boot information reports their real addresses (`Kernel::with_secret_and_memory`; `BOOTMEM` lines; host `tests/bootmem.rs`, plants `plant_mem_identity`, `plant_mem_overlap_ok`, `plant_mem_no_guard`). **STILL OPEN, at the granularity of the mechanism:** (a) the untyped frames are not BACKED by real mappings (the kernel keeps their contents in its model), so this says which memory the kernel treats as its own, not that a ring-3 task can touch it; (b) [PARTLY CLOSED 2026-10-31, see the ROWS-work stream note at the end of this cell] the root task does not yet RECEIVE the encoded boot information, read-only, in its own address space, nor build a child from its untyped memory through syscalls in ring 3 (needs the kernel-gate dispatcher, / F28a); (c) [CLOSED 2026-10-25, `f67fcb17f` and `77df8e3fc`: the default UEFI image builds ONE Kernel; the self-test kernel and the `kernel::boot` composition are behind the feature `boot_selftest`, default off, and `boot_stack_depth` asserts that rule] the image still also runs a separate boot self-test kernel and the `kernel::boot` scheduling composition on their own `Kernel` instances (to move behind a feature); (d) [CLOSED 2026-10-25, `c730477e7`: the AArch64 image boots a root task over the device tree's real RAM, QEMU virt EL1 and EL2, VM only] the AArch64 image is not covered by this unit; (e) bare metal is unverified (a x86-64 test server window item). **ROWS-work stream note, 2026-10-31 (main `5a3868e7e`):** item (b) is now MOSTLY done. `bootinfo_scene` (commit `b02880e9b`, VM = QEMU TCG): the boot information is encoded into ONE page mapped user-READABLE with the writable bit cleared on its 4 KiB leaf; a root program reads it (a fold and the header word), a gate call that names the page as an OUT pointer is refused INVALID, and a Task is retyped out of the untyped capability the information names, through the kernel gate; `proofs/bootinfo_uefi_proof.py` normal 12/12, plant `bootinfo_plant_writable` convicted 4/4. RE-RUN 2026-10-31 by the ROWS work stream on main `3fec2ba3a` (QEMU TCG, VM): `bootinfo_uefi_proof.py` normal 12/12. The two calls that finish the job are WIRED gate rows (`9f09a324a`, `microkernel/src/gate_taskcfg.rs`): `task_admit` 140 (the spawn authority releases the freshly retyped task, optionally into a sandbox partition) and `task_set_cspace` 141 (the schedule authority gives it a CSpace root); HOST tests `tests/gate_taskcfg.rs` (5 tests, 2 plants `plant_gate_taskcfg_noobject` / `plant_gate_taskcfg_norights`), and `synthyrion_gatecases` success and refusal scripts for both rows run from ring 3 by the gate-table scene (`gate_table_proof.py`; RE-MEASURED 2026-10-31 on main `3fec2ba3a`, QEMU TCG, VM: both rows pass from ring 3, success script and all refusals; see F28a for the whole run). **ROOT-PATH ADDRESS SPACE (2026-11-01, branch f24_bundle; gap (a) and the child-address-space gap CLOSED at VM strength):** five WIRED gate rows, all by pointer in the caller's own CSpace. `task_set_vspace` 142 (schedule authority; places a task the root retyped and admitted CONFINED in a space of its own whose ROOT TABLE IS A FRAME the root retyped; an armed kernel with no partition schedule answers DENIED with the root to schedule in `value`), `vspace_map_frame` 143 (a retyped frame is mapped read-only or writable, never executable, into a task that owns a space; the kernel's tree and a parked task are refused; with the root's own Task capability it maps into the root's OWN space), `frame_freeze` 146 (drops a frame's write right for good; WRITE_XOR_EXECUTE while a writable mapping is live), `vspace_map_exec` 147 (a FROZEN frame is mapped read + execute, never writable; the map-exec authority is a Task capability on `EXEC_MAP_OBJECT` with MAP and EXECUTE, which the boot installs in the root's CNode; looked up by the one helper `exec_auth_at`, which F27A's row 87 shares) and `vspace_donate_table` 148 (a retyped frame becomes a page-table page of a space). CHARGED TABLES (B1): a placed space is born in supplied mode, so every table page, root table included, is a frame retyped from the root's untyped memory; a map the donated pages cannot cover is EXHAUSTED and installs nothing; a donated page is never a data mapping; a dying table frame tears its space down through the kernel's one disposal path and its tasks are reaped (test + plant). KERNEL CR3: `Kernel::set_space_executor` (schedule authority) installs the arch layer's executor and `dispatch_core` calls it with the dispatched task's REAL root, so the KERNEL, not the loader, loads CR3; `Kernel::boot_give_space` gives the root task a space of its own too. HOST: `microkernel/tests/gate_vspace.rs`, `gate_vspace_exec.rs`, `gate_vspace_tables.rs` (12 tests) and gatecases scripts for every row; plants `plant_vspace_shares_root`, `plant_vspace_no_split_check`, `plant_vspace_map_kernel_space`, `plant_vspace_exec_noauth`, `plant_vspace_freeze_noop`, `plant_vspace_wx`, `plant_vspace_table_uncharged`, `plant_vspace_table_unowned` (a table from a frame without the write right), `plant_vspace_table_death_keeps_space`, `plant_dispatch_stale_cr3`, `plant_boot_space_noauth` each turn a test RED (`plant_vspace_wx` removes the frozen check together with the live-writer check: the live-writer check alone is unreachable by any input, a frozen frame has no writable mapping and a frame with one cannot be frozen). VM (QEMU TCG/OVMF, not bare metal): `boot_uefi --features rootas_scene` (`src/rootas_scene.rs`, `proofs/rootas_uefi_proof.py`), normal 36/36: the kernel is built over REAL memory; the boot gives the ring-3 root a space of its own from retyped frames; the root, through the gate only, retypes a Task, the child's root table and table pages and four data/code frames, admits the Task confined, installs the partition schedule, is refused a map before the table pages are donated (EXHAUSTED) and succeeds after; it maps its code frame writable into ITS OWN space and COPIES THE CHILD IMAGE THROUGH THAT MAPPING AT CPL 3 (the frame's real memory equals the image; the arch layer only executes the kernel's tree for the root, `SEL_SYNC`, and writes no code byte), unmaps it, is refused an exec map while unfrozen (NOT_FROZEN) and a freeze under a live writer (WRITE_XOR_EXECUTE), freezes it, is refused a writable map (FROZEN) and an exec map with a MAP-only authority (DENIED), and maps it read + execute for the child; the child's four page tables are the root's retyped frames, written from the kernel's tree; the KERNEL loads the child's root table into CR3 at the dispatch; the child runs at CPL 3 from the executable frame, reads a marker through its read-only mapping, writes a pattern found in its data frame's real memory, round-trips its stack, and FAULTS (#PF read back by vector, CR2 and error code) on an unmapped address, on a write to the read-only page and on a read of kernel memory. Plants `rootas_plant_userleaf`, `rootas_plant_rowrite`, `rootas_plant_sharedroot`, `rootas_plant_exec_noauth`, `rootas_plant_nofreeze`, `rootas_plant_tableuncharged`, `rootas_plant_stalecr3` and `rootas_plant_nosync` are each convicted. HARDWARE TABLES, L1 + L1b + L2 (2026-11-02): `Kernel::freeze_kernel_half` (schedule authority, ONCE; present, supervisor-only, no huge bit) freezes the supervisor half, and the KERNEL, not the loader, puts exactly those entries into the root table of every space it builds (`real_table_image`), refusing any user map into one of those PML4 slots; the ring-3 root runs on the boot image, so `boot_give_space` freezes its boot-image half with its space. `Kernel::set_table_hooks` installs the arch layer's `TableSync` writer and `TableRead` reader: the kernel hands the writer its complete hardware image on every map, on row-92 unmap and when a space is placed or given (no request exists any more), and `dispatch_core` reads every table page of the task's space back through the reader and compares it with its own image before a core is handed the task (every entry exact, every other slot zero): a user bit added, a missing supervisor entry or an extra entry REFUSES the dispatch (`dispatch_refusals`). `Kernel::seal_image_pages` freezes a snapshot of the boot-image table pages under the boot-image half (the firmware's PDPT and PDs) and the same dispatch check compares them with it. The legacy `MapMemory` / `Unmap` syscalls refuse a charged space. HOST: `tests/gate_vspace_hw.rs` (5 tests). VM (QEMU TCG/OVMF, not bare metal), `rootas_scene` normal 40/40: the kernel freezes its supervisor half and takes the table writer, seals the boot-image pages, the child's root table already holds the kernel's frozen entry before anything of the loader's, and a supervisor half tampered with (a user bit) and a boot-image PD entry tampered with (one bit) are each REFUSED at dispatch and accepted again once restored. ONE PLANT SWEEP ON ONE SHA (`4fe5956d34ac479d1e3d638f51df2cc2778ab820`, the unit as queued: after the merge of main (F8 rows 144/145), the ArchHooks refactor, L1/L1b/L2, the boot_feature_sets line, the CR0.WP window check, the census-clean `freeze_kernel_half` and the removal of the dead `VSPACE_ROOT_BASE` constant (the reserved-id predicate test `reserved_mint` 3/3)): host, every plant run against its test file, normal green and plant RED: gate_vspace (4) `plant_vspace_shares_root` 3 failed, `plant_vspace_no_split_check` 3, `plant_vspace_map_kernel_space` 2; gate_vspace_exec (3) `plant_vspace_exec_noauth` 1, `plant_vspace_freeze_noop` 2, `plant_vspace_wx` 2; gate_vspace_tables (5) `plant_vspace_table_uncharged` 2, `plant_vspace_table_unowned` 1, `plant_dispatch_stale_cr3` 1, `plant_vspace_table_death_keeps_space` 1, `plant_boot_space_noauth` 1; gate_vspace_hw (5) `plant_kernel_half_user_ok` 1, `plant_table_sync_skipped` 4, `plant_dispatch_unverified` 2, `plant_legacy_map_charged_ok` 1, `plant_image_pages_unchecked` 1. QEMU: normal 41/41, userleaf 3/3, rowrite 3/3, sharedroot 3/3, execnoauth 3/3, nofreeze 4/4, tableuncharged 3/3, stalecr3 3/3, syncskipped 3/3, unverified 3/3, imgunchecked 3/3, wpleft 3/3 (a plant scenario passes only when its named checks are convicted; under `userleaf` / `rowrite` the LOADER's table writer is the faulty party and the kernel's dispatch check catches it; under `syncskipped` the hardware tables never reach the first dispatch of the root, which is refused). **LATER, never claimed here:** (1) a Hyper-V Generation 2 boot of the packaged VHDX (`launch_hyperv.ps1`) on a host with the Hyper-V role: the development machine is Windows 11 Home (`Get-Command Get-VM` finds nothing, edition Core), a host-capability limit, owner ruling 2026-11-07; the guest-side Hyper-V interface (`synthyrion_hvguest`, `hv_scene`, VM strength under QEMU/KVM `hv_*` flags) stays as already recorded. Needs a Windows Pro/Enterprise/Server host, then `launch_hyperv.ps1` plus a serial capture of the kernel's PASS lines and a plant (a VHDX with the loader removed must not print them). (2) the release SIGNATURE of the image set: every file of the set (images, launchers, `f26_kit.ps1`) is in `SHA512SUMS`, so the existing release path (`synthyrion_export sign`, ML-DSA-87, release vault) covers them; it is made at release time by the owner with the release key, not by an agent, and these evidence images are a dev build of main, not a release. The kit states "UNSIGNED" until then. No installer by design: the set is bootable media (raw/qcow2/VHDX/VMDK/OVA) plus the one-command kit; QEMU is outside software (GPL-2.0) and is not bundled. Signature note: the 0.1.3 signing covers the image set including the kit; until that signing HAPPENS the signature stays LATER and the kit states UNSIGNED. **Previous gap text (2026-11-04 form, kept for the record; where it says OPEN or one remaining step, the sentence above supersedes it):** **Hyper-V boot: OPEN, test later.** A Hyper-V Generation 2 boot has NOT been run: the development machine is Windows 11 Home (no Hyper-V;, owner 2026-10-22: leave it open until a Pro machine exists). Everything else for the row is built (VHDX, WSL launcher, and a reproducible build: two builds of the x86-64 UEFI boot image in separate target directories give the same SHA-512, so do two builds of the release loader; measured 2026-11-10 after a linker fix; the check is not repeated on a second machine and the other ports are not checked). **Guest-side Hyper-V interface (2026-11-02, VM strength: QEMU/KVM with `hv_*` flags in WSL2, NOT Microsoft's Hyper-V):** `synthyrion_hvguest` (the CPUID `0x4000_0000..` leaves, guest OS id and hypercall MSRs, hypercall page; 14 host tests, 3 mutations RED) and `boot_uefi` feature `hv_scene` (`hwrun/hv_guest_wsl.py`): Synthyrion recognises the interface, maps its hypercall page executable through a private page-table copy and a real `HvCallNotifyLongSpinWait` through it returns success; controls and plants convicted (no `hv_*` flags: WrongVendor `KVMKVMKVM`; page mapped NX: PageNotExecutable before any MSR write; a stock Linux with `kvm=off` reads the same leaves as Microsoft Hyper-V). Remaining gap, one step: a Generation 2 boot of the VHDX on a host with the Hyper-V role (decision; no Windows image is needed). The set is unsigned; no installer

### Gap or limit, in full

Strength: **VM (QEMU virtio-serial device model on WHPX), not hardware (B19)**. **The signed COMMAND is proven end to end (2026-11-02):** the default image's `Responder` carries `synthyrion_guestlink::sink::RemoteCmdSink`, which hands the COMMAND envelope to `synthyrion_remotecmd` (the box chooses the challenge; ML-DSA-87 over challenge + command, context `Synthyrion-REMOTE-COMMAND-v1`; five 1024-byte signature chunks, so `MAX_PAYLOAD` is 1032; a challenge is retired by every decision). Host: `tests/command_sink.rs` 11 tests (accepted; tampered, unsigned, another key, replay and no-anchor each refused with their own verdict; malformed = `ERROR(BAD_PAYLOAD)`; the proof anchor file is the key of the public seed), plant `plant_sink_unverified` turns 5 RED. VM (`virtcon_default_proof.py`, evidence `tools/synthyrion_export/evidence/virtcon_command_2026-11-02/`): `command` 9/9 on an image built with the PUBLIC proof anchor (`virtcon_proof_anchor`; never in a shipped set): `synthyrion_guestctl signed` is ACCEPTED and returns the command's output, `signed-tampered` REFUSED verdict 5, `signed-unsigned` verdict 4, `signed-wrongkey` verdict 5, `signed-replay` first accepted then REFUSED, and the box's own counters read 2 accepted / 4 refused; `command_noanchor` 4/4: the DEFAULT image carries NO anchor and refuses a validly signed command with verdict 1 (NoAnchor); plant `virtcon_plant_sink_unverified` convicted (the refusal checks go RED). The same image still passes `normal` 8/8 (`mode=msix irqs=257 rescued=0`) and `regress` 10/10 (verdict lines identical to the baseline). **Limits of the COMMAND proof:** the shipped image has no release anchor yet (the release signing process supplies it; until then every COMMAND answers NoAnchor), the allow-list is one word (`version`), and the proof anchor is a public test key. Proven: the frame protocol (host tests), the driver's virtqueues and control state machine (host tests with plants, VM), the QEMU proof with a host client (VM, scene and default image), interrupt-driven receive with polling fallback (host tests + VM with two plants), and the unchanged verdict (VM, before/after). **Stated limits (owner ruling of the project lead, 2026-10-31: recorded, not worked):** (1) **no vsock path** (a different transport, not a defect of the console one); (2) **one data port** (a second `DEVICE_ADD` is refused by name); (3) **no `VIRTIO_CONSOLE_F_SIZE` / `F_EMERG_WRITE`** (optional features); (4) **no other hypervisor's console device tested** (QEMU's model only); (5) **VM, not bare metal**; (6) the idle window spins with interrupts enabled (no HLT), one MSI-X vector is shared by every queue, and the service patches the live IDT in place (checked: refused if the table cannot take the vector); (7) `hwrun` (silicon hold) builds keep their own final state and do not start the service. ## § 3 Complete, with a stated limit (5)

OPEN: bare-metal ARM boot on silicon.

### Gap or limit, in full

OPEN: bare-metal ARM boot on silicon. ASSESSED 2026-10-21 (read-only, as root): (a) NO kexec: the NAS kernel has `# CONFIG_KEXEC is not set`, no `kexec` binary, no `/proc/sys/kernel/kexec*`; (b) the only ways to see a result are the SoC UART (16550A at 0x98007800, needs a serial cable the development machine does not have) or a result word read back by Linux after a warm return, which needs (a); (c) without kexec the only way to run our kernel is to replace the image the boot loader starts (`bootdev=mmc bootpart=3`: the eMMC), a persistent write that is forbidden. So it was NOT attempted. Also needed before any silicon boot: a platform port (RTD1619B: GICv3 redistributor at 0xff140000, 16550 UART not PL011, RAM from 0x01040000, PSCI over SMC) and an ARM board with a serial console (the Raspberry Pi 5 purchase)

OPEN: (a) RISC-V silicon, none owned; QEMU TCG does not model caches, so fence.i/cache maintenance and the loader's hand-off are unproven on real hardware; (b) USER TASKS ON EVERY HART…

### Gap or limit, in full

OPEN: (a) RISC-V silicon, none owned; QEMU TCG does not model caches, so `fence.i`/cache maintenance and the loader's hand-off are unproven on real hardware; (b) USER TASKS ON EVERY HART (2026-10-30, VM strength, QEMU TCG with OpenSBI; unit rv_smp): all 4 harts run U-mode tasks at once. Each hart has its OWN root and level-1 page table (a copy of the boot hart's; the level-0 tables beneath are shared), its own trap stack with its id at the top (the trap stub restores `tp` from it), its own saved kernel context and first frame; every entry from U-mode takes the big kernel lock; a task has an `on_hart` field so the picker never hands a task to a second hart; a hart with nothing to run waits in `wfi` and is woken by an IPI from the hart that releases a task. The run is the single-hart run's seven programs (ping/pong over capabilities, store and exec faults answered by a user supervisor, a capability-less task, a hog preempted by the timer) plus four roll-callers that cannot exit until all four harts have run user code. Checks (all PASS on `-smp 4`, 106 of 106 scenarios and 5 live ones still pass): `smp-user-tasks-run-on-every-hart` (each hart loaded a task and handled ecalls from user code; one full run: loads 7/6/2/10, ecalls from user code 25/11/1/31; the split varies run to run, every hart is above zero by construction), `smp-a-task-never-runs-on-two-harts-at-once` (no task was loaded while another hart ran it, and every trap came from the hart the table named as owner), `smp-user-run-every-task-exited-with-its-expected-status`, `smp-per-hart-page-tables-hold-no-writable-executable-entry` (6,660 entries over the per-hart copies in the full run, 0 violations). Plants RED by name: `plant_user_boot_hart_only` (the other harts never enter), `plant_user_double_run` (the picker lets a task run on two harts). STILL OPEN in (b): there is no per-hart run queue or affinity (the kernel's one scheduler is used under the big lock and tasks migrate freely), the big lock is the only lock (fine-grained locking is the F23 stance), 4 harts on QEMU `virt` only, the user programs are the test programs (no loader for user binaries), and no silicon; (c) PLIC (2026-10-30, VM strength, QEMU `virt` map): the PLIC is driven (S-mode context 2h+1, priority, enable, threshold, claim, complete); an external interrupt is claimed, the source masked at the PLIC, noted, completed, and a real device line (UART0 transmit-empty, source 10) is tested to a bound notification that wakes a blocked driver (check `plic-device-interrupts-claimed-masked-and-completed`, live scenario, plants `plant_plic_no_enable` and `plant_plic_no_complete`). STILL OPEN: only the boot hart's context is routed (no per-hart device affinity), sources 1..31 only, the PLIC addresses are the QEMU `virt` constants (not read from the device tree, which this EDK2 does not publish), MSI/AIA is not implemented; (d) ASIDs (2026-10-30, VM strength; unit rv_smp): every user task runs under its own `satp.ASID` from a pool (`synthyrion_rv64::asid`, host-tested: 6 tests, plant `plant_asid_recycle_clean` turns 3 RED). The ASID width is read from `satp` (measured 16 bits on QEMU). A context switch writes the user slot and then `satp` with the incoming task's number and executes NO global `sfence.vma` (all post-boot fences go through two counting functions); a number that changes owner (11 tasks over a pool of 6: 13 recycles in the full run) is flushed on this hart and, through the SBI RFENCE extension (`remote_sfence_vma_asid`), on every hart that ran it, before the new owner runs (13 shootdowns, 0 failures). Checks: `asid-satp-carries-the-incoming-tasks-asid-on-every-switch-and-no-two-running-tasks-share-one` (read back from `satp`), `asid-context-switch-executes-no-global-sfence-vma` (0 global fences inside any switch), `asid-recycled-numbers-are-shot-down-on-every-hart-that-ran-them`, `asid-reuse-flushes-every-hart-that-cached-it` (a direct probe: all 4 harts cache a translation under one ASID, the boot hart retargets the mapping with no fence and still reads the OLD word, then the recycle flush runs and every hart reads the NEW word), `asid-width-probed-from-satp`. Plants RED by name: `plant_asid_no_shootdown`, `plant_asid_local_only`, `plant_asid_pool_clean`, `plant_user_asid_zero`, `plant_switch_global_fence`. STATED LIMIT, MEASURED: QEMU's TCG TLB does not tag by ASID. It flushes everything on every `satp` write and on every `sfence.vma` whatever its ASID operand (the boot prints `tlb-model: entry-survives-an-asid-switch=no entry-survives-a-fence-of-another-asid=no`), so a translation SURVIVING a switch to another ASID cannot be observed in the VM; what the VM shows is the kernel's own behaviour (which number it loads, which fences it runs, which harts it reaches) and the stale-then-flushed semantics above. The retention itself is for ASID-tagged silicon (none owned). STILL OPEN in (d): Sv39 only (no Sv48/Sv57), the pool is 6 numbers in this image on purpose (so the recycle path runs; it would be sized to the probed width), kernel mappings are not marked global (`G`), a hart whose ASIDLEN is 0 would need a global-flush fallback that is not built, and no silicon; (e) **hypervisor extension (2026-11-02, work stream F25D, VM strength: QEMU 11.0.50 TCG with `-cpu rv64,h=true`, not silicon; `boot_riscv64/src/hext.rs`, encodings and verdict in `synthyrion_rv64::hext`, written from the Privileged specification):** the kernel in HS-mode reads `hstatus` (VSXL = 2), writes `hgatp` (Sv39x4, VMID 1) and reads it back, builds a three-level G-stage table with the checked constructors (16 KiB root, leaves with U set, W^X), `sret`s a trivial guest into VS-mode, the guest's store reaches its data page through the G-stage and its load from an unmapped guest-physical address takes the designed load guest-page fault (scause 21, `htval` = GPA >> 2, `hstatus.SPV` and `GVA` set, `sepc` the load) which the kernel judges and returns from. Plants: `hgatp` installed over an empty table and G-stage leaves without U each turn the check RED (scause 20 at the first fetch); a hart started with `h=false` prints a SKIP and boots. **Step 2 (same day, VM strength): two-stage translation and a virtual interrupt.** The guest runs with its OWN `vsatp` (an Sv39 table in guest memory, built with `synthyrion_rv64`'s checked PTE constructors, over the G-stage): its store through both stages reaches the data page; a miss in ITS table (hedeleg bit 13 hands the VS-stage page fault to the guest) is taken by the guest's own handler as scause 13 with the guest virtual address in `stval`; a G-stage miss behind a valid VS mapping exits to HS as the guest page fault (scause 21, `htval` = GPA >> 2, `stval` = the guest VIRTUAL address, so the two stages are told apart); and a virtual supervisor software interrupt injected through `hvip` (delegated by `hideleg`) is delivered to the guest's handler, which reads scause = SSIP, clears it and returns (`hext2-*` checks, judged by pure functions in `synthyrion_rv64::hext`). Plants: the guest's VS tables left out of the G-stage turn every translated step RED (the first walk is an instruction guest-page fault at the table's guest-physical page, scause 20); an `hvip` injection never delegated is never seen by the guest and only the interrupt check goes RED. **Step 3 (same day, VM strength): the RV64 VCPU kernel object and a VMM loop (F22 on RISC-V).** `microkernel` `vcpu/rv.rs`: the same capability model as the x86 VCPU (`rvcpu_create` / `rvcpu_new_unbound` / `rvcpu_bind` / `rvcpu_start` / register access / `rvcpu_inject` through `hvip` needing WRITE / `rvcpu_enter` / `rvcpu_exited` / `rvcpu_poll`), the G-stage root being the existing EPT object, a guest trap becoming a `FaultKind::VmExit` fault message to the VMM, a stale guest page fault resuming without a message; 12 host tests whose names mirror the x86 ones (`tests/vcpu_rv.rs`). `boot_riscv64/src/rvm.rs` is the arch layer (the kernel's G-stage model turned into the Sv39x4 table, `hgatp`/`hideleg`/`hvip`, `sret` with the guest registers) and a minimal VMM loop that serves ONE exit kind, the guest's SBI `ecall` (`scause` 10): the guest takes an `hvip`-injected VSSIP in its own handler, asks the SBI base extension for the specification version, prints through the legacy console call and shuts down; the VMM collects `IOK` in 6 fault messages and the shutdown reply stops the VCPU. Plants: a VCPU started unbound and an injection through a READ-only capability each turn their named check RED (host test and QEMU scene). Found live and fixed: the guest's `sp`, `gp` and `tp` cannot be loaded (the trap stub and the compiled kernel run on the kernel's), and the VS-level CSRs of a previous guest must be reset or a pending virtual interrupt is taken into the previous guest's handler. **Step 4 (2026-11-04, work stream F26, units f25d_rvretype 64300 (landed as 81acfbd9e), f25d_rvgate 97300 (commit 1f464a5ae) and f25d_stack 1100 (commit 045394a86, after rvgate); VM strength: QEMU TCG with `-cpu rv64,h=true` and sstc, NOT silicon):** the RISC-V VCPU is made by `retype` (gate `retype` selector 48 with `RETYPE_KIND_VCPU_FLAG` and family 3, one frame, born unbound, revoked with its untyped region; unit rvretype); the VCPU calls are ring-3 gate rows 149 and 152-155 (unit rvgate; evidence on the exact tip: the ring-3 gate table passes 991 of 991 typed calls, and rows 149 and 152-155 pass all 27 of their success and refusal lines; golden, header and client regenerated); and unit stack adds the per-VCPU save of the VS-level CSRs, the VS external/virtual interrupt, the VS timer through sstc (`vstimecmp`) and the guest register context (the guest's `sp`, `gp` and `tp` are the guest's own, not the kernel's). Evidence on the exact stack tip: 47 of 47 host tests, 34 rv plants each RED for their designed reason, the judge `run_qemu.sh -smp 1` with no FAIL, and a fresh unsafe-confinement record. STATED LIMITS (what COMPLETE does not claim;: RISC-V silicon is LATER, none is owned): one guest context per hart (one guest at a time per hart; the scenes run one hart); a RISC-V guest is entered by the kernel's arch layer, not by a ring-3 VMM on a real hart; the preemption of a spinning guest is delivered to the arch layer, not to the VMM as a fault message; there is no ring-3 row for the VS CSR / `vstimecmp` image; no stock Linux guest on RISC-V; no RISC-V silicon, and QEMU TCG models no caches; (f) PMP: the PMP registers (`pmpcfg`, `pmpaddr`) are machine-mode CSRs, so an S-mode kernel cannot configure them; OpenSBI, the trusted M-mode firmware, does. Synthyrion now VERIFIES THE EFFECT at boot (VM strength, QEMU TCG): the check `pmp-denies-the-s-mode-load-of-the-opensbi-region` maps OpenSBI's first megapage read-only for one probe and requires a load ACCESS fault (scause 5), then unmaps it; plant `plant_pmp_probe_wrong` turns it RED. STATED LIMIT: Synthyrion configuring PMP itself would need Synthyrion to be the M-mode firmware, which is not built; (g) this EDK2 does not publish the device tree in the configuration table, so the loader falls back to QEMU's top-of-RAM slot and a validated page search (measured, commented in the loader); (h) no proof or binary-level validation covers RISC-V (no Sail model wired), and the new roots are outside `suitectl unsafe`'s population like `boot_aarch64`; (i) a bare `cargo check` of the two roots needs `RUSTC_BOOTSTRAP=1` (B18)

**2026-11-19 CLOSED: EVERY INTERRUPT AND EXCEPTION ENTRY FROM RING 3, IN BOTH IMAGES; ROW COMPLETE (unit SPECTRE-IDT, unit 2 of the Spectre-v2 work, owner ruling = A; NOT silicon; static order and register state only, per):** WHAT IS BUILT: on a legacy-IBRS processor every IDT stub writes IA32_SPEC_CTRL before its first call or indirect branch, with the same write text and guard as the system-call entries: an INTERRUPT stub when the CS the CPU pushed has RPL 3 (a context already runs with the register set; the only kernel code that runs with it clear is an exit window, and every exit sequence now masks interrupts first), an EXCEPTION stub on every entry whatever the CPL (the NMI, #MC and #DF on their IST stacks, and a fault inside an exit window, can arrive at CPL 0 with the register clear). Before an `iretq` whose frame has RPL 3 the register is cleared, with interrupts masked; a return to CPL 0 leaves it set. Nesting never corrupts the state (the only state is the register; every path ends with a clear immediately before a return to CPL 3, or leaves it set for ring 0); the one imprecision is on the safe side and is stated: an NMI or exception taken between an exit write and its `iretq` returns to CPL 0 with the register set, so that ring-3 slice runs with IBRS set until its next entry. No stub uses `swapgs` or a GS-relative access (there is none in either image), so no swapgs ordering applies. Where IA32_SPEC_CTRL is absent nothing is written and the plan names the exposure. No retpoline build (it needs a nightly-only compiler flag). The write is ONE text, two assembly sequences, expanded in the HAL's three `syscall` entry stubs (`synthyrion_syscall_stub`, `synthyrion_syscall_stub_r3`, `synthyrion_gate_stub`), in the first drop to ring 3 (`synthyrion_enter_ring3`) and in the domain host's `int` gate and domain entry (`isr_dom`, `dom_enter`); the skip a processor takes when it needs no per-entry write lands on an LFENCE. Nested KVM can itself intercept or emulate RDTSC, so the 18-cycle native floor is the nested host's floor, not silicon's. Residual timing channels (a guest loop's iterations per slice, a counting thread on a second vCPU) are NOT closed. THE DESIGN: with RDTSC exiting on, the guest's TSC is the VMM's one virtual clock (`synthyrion_vmhost::timemodel::guest_tsc`: the virtual time in guest TSC cycles, never the hardware counter), advanced by `DEFAULT_NS_PER_EXIT` (1,000 ns) per exit at the guest's 2 GHz (`GUEST_TSC_KHZ` 2,000,000), so two consecutive trapped reads differ by at least the GRANULE G = 2,000 cycles (1 us), which is not a tunable but what the VMM already assumed, pinned by test; G is about 7 to 10 times the cache hit versus miss gap, and the guest cannot time one access with its TSC. `hypervisor::runloop::enable_rdtsc_exiting` sets primary control bit 12 and refuses, leaving the list untouched, on a CPU that forbids it. EVIDENCE. Host tests, each RED first against a stub: `tsc_granule.rs` 5 (RED 5 of 5; plant `plant_tsc_host_leak` convicted by 3 tests), `rdtsc_exiting.rs` 4 (RED 3 of 4; plant `plant_rdtsc_not_intercepted` convicted by 3), `tsc_probe_verdict.rs` 6 (RED 5 of 6; plant `plant_tsc_verdict_lenient` convicted by 2), `tsc_probe_parse.rs` 3 (RED 2 of 3); the verdict function pre-states its thresholds before any run (the native control must read under 500 cycles or NO claim is made; a trapped run needs every pair different and a least delta of at least 2,000). the build VM nested KVM, the probe guest reading RDTSC 20,001 times back to back on the HAL demo run loop (`synthyrion_hal::demo_vmx_tsc_probe`, scene `tsc_scene`): NATIVE CONTROL (RDTSC exiting off): least nonzero delta 18 cycles, 20,000 nonzero deltas, 0 RDTSC exits, `NativeControlOk` (the control's floor was stated BEFORE the trapped run); TRAPPED: least nonzero delta 2,000 cycles, 20,000 nonzero deltas, 20,001 RDTSC exits of 20,045 VM exits, `TrappedOk`; the two plants built into the image, `plant_tsc_loop_not_trapped` (the demo loop silently does not set the trap) and `plant_rdtsc_not_intercepted` (the enable returns Ok and leaves the bit clear), each read the native clock (18 cycles, 0 RDTSC exits) and are convicted by the scene (`NotTrapped`, `TSC-SCENE-FAIL`). STILL OPEN for the timestamp-counter gap: bare metal, the residual channels above, and the `smp_virtual_tsc` Linux guest (1 of 4 historic runs; its failing mechanism is to be NAMED before any stated limit). is NOT closed and NO confidentiality claim is made. **2026-11-16: smp_virtual_tsc TIMEOUT MECHANISM NAMED AND FIXED (a queued submission, main e92da0de7; VM strength; status stays PARTIAL).** WHAT IS SHOWN, AND ONLY THIS: the two-CPU Linux scene built with the diagnostic feature `smp_virtual_tsc` (trapped RDTSC, the one virtual clock) reached PASS in 10 of 10 runs on the build VM nested KVM after the fix, each in 6 to 8 minutes under the unchanged 1,500 s limit; before it, 0 of 2 runs passed (both TIMEOUT at the identical guest point, 25 minutes each, `Marking TSC unstable` count 0). This is a COUNT on one nested-KVM host, not a reliability proof and not bare metal; N=10 was fixed before the runs. THE MECHANISM (named from the two captured failing runs, not from the 11-04 hypothesis): the VMM had no CMOS clock, so ports 0x70 and 0x71 were unclaimed and read as all ones (`synthyrion_vmhost::bus`); register 0x0A therefore read UIP (update in progress) set FOREVER and Linux waited out its whole one-second timeout on each of three RTC accesses at boot (guest log `Unable to read current time from RTC` at 1.262 s). With RDTSC trapped the guest clock advances only about 1.3 microseconds of virtual time per exit, so one 1 s timeout costs about 750,000 exits; the build VM serves about 1,000 trapped exits per second (about 1 ms each), so the boot could not finish inside the limit. Evidence of the mechanism: the guest log line above, the BSP alternating between `rtc_cmos_read` and `delay_tsc` (heartbeat RIPs symbolised with the guest kernel's System.map 7.0.0-42-generic), the AP idle in `pv_native_safe_halt`, 1.45M exits at virtual 1.93 s with CPU 1 at about 4,700 exits. THE FIX: `synthyrion_vmhost::cmos`, a CMOS RTC on ports 0x70 and 0x71 (index with the NMI mask bit ignored, UIP never set, binary 24-hour mode, VRT set, interrupt flags and battery bit read-only), FROZEN at the one fixed instant 2026-01-01 00:00:00 with NO host clock read, so no host wall time reaches the guest and two runs read the same value; `Vmm::install_cmos`; the Linux scene installs it and its verdict now REQUIRES that the guest log no `Unable to read current time from RTC` (`rtc_ok`). EVIDENCE. Host tests `tests/cmos_rtc.rs` 6, RED first against a stub that answers 0xFF (4 of 6 RED: `register_a_never_reports_update_in_progress`, `the_time_reads_back_as_the_one_fixed_instant`, `the_index_write_latches_and_ignores_the_nmi_mask_bit`, `on_the_bus_the_ports_are_claimed_and_register_a_is_not_all_ones`; the plant test and the frozen-clock test pass on the stub by design), GREEN 6 of 6; `synthyrion_vmhost` suite 217 passed 0 failed. the build VM nested KVM on the unit commit 0ea1440f4: one-CPU scene PASS with `RTC=true`; plant `cmosstuck` (register 0x0A reads UIP forever, what the unclaimed bus gave) CONVICTED BY A BOOT (guest log `Unable to read current time from RTC` at 0.103 s and 0.238 s, `RTC=false`, `LINUX-SCENE-FAIL`); default two-CPU scene (no virtual TSC, the shipped configuration) PASS with `RTC=true`; two-CPU `--smp --trace --virtual-tsc --limit 1500` 10 of 10 PASS, every run `RTC=true`, the clocksource `tsc` and stable to the end, a 2000 ms sleep measured 2000 ms (8 runs) or 2010 ms (2 runs) on the guest clock, frequency from CPUID, `Marking TSC unstable` count 0 in all ten. The landed tip 25d88cefe adds one comment (a sast marker arguing the const-eval panic at linux_scene.rs line 79); the the build VM runs were NOT repeated on the landed tree; the queue re-proved census (ACCEPTED), sast 0 critical 0 warning, `boot_feature_sets` 12 of 12. microkernel/src is untouched. NOT CLAIMED: (1) `smp_virtual_tsc` stays a DIAGNOSTIC feature and OFF in every release feature set; nothing here turns it on. (2) The 11-04 failures (runs 43 and 44, the clocksource watchdog marking the TSC unstable after virtual-time leaps) are NOT explained by this fix: they did not recur in these ten runs and their mechanism stays unnamed. (5) The one-CPU control also logs one `Marking TSC unstable` line (the one-CPU guest TSC is the host TSC), unchanged and not part of this item. **2026-11-16: T3 (live branch-target-injection leakage) CLOSED AS A STATED LIMIT (project lead decision; status stays PARTIAL).** WHAT IS SHOWN: T1, static: the first host code after every VM exit (`synthyrion_vm_exit_prologue`) contains zero indirect branches (the static gate, budget 0, with a planted indirect call convicted); T2, register state: IA32_SPEC_CTRL is read back at the first host instruction after each exit and at each entry and matches the plan (for example 215,743 of 215,743 exits with 0 mismatches in a the build VM two-CPU scene run, with the plants that skip the IBRS write, the guest-value restore and the return-stack fill each convicted by a boot). WHAT IS NOT SHOWN: live branch-target-injection leakage is NOT demonstrated, and no exploit or positive-control harness was built; the mitigation is verified by T1 and T2 state only, not by observing a leak stop. Efficacy on silicon (including parts with enhanced IBRS) stays under the hardware items and is NOT shown. THE JUDGE: `synthyrion_vmhost::btiprobe` (main 27fd9b666, a queued submission) is a pure threshold function with host tests and a plant; it is landed and deliberately UNUSED by any image, and stays so; nothing calls it and no claim rests on it. **2026-11-16: SSBD AND BHI LANDED, RETPOLINE A STATED LIMIT (a queued submission, main f1eef3901; VM strength for SSBD, HOST MODEL for BHI; status stays PARTIAL).** WHAT IS SHOWN, BY REGISTER STATE, NEVER BY A LEAK: (1) SSBD (speculative store bypass disable, `IA32_SPEC_CTRL` bit 2, CPUID.(7,0):EDX[31]) is ON in the host whenever it is enumerated (policy, security first; the owner may overrule it to a per-domain policy). It rides in the SPEC_CTRL writes the VM transition already makes (legacy-IBRS exit: IBRS and SSBD, entry restore 0; eIBRS: IBRS and SSBD at the entry), so it adds NO new MSR write. On the build VM nested KVM (a legacy-IBRS Haswell that enumerates SSBD) the register readback at the first host instruction after every exit and just before every entry matched the plan in 3 of 3 normal runs (about 96,000 exits each, 0 mismatches). PLANTS, each convicted by a boot: the write leaving SSBD out (`plant_spec2_skip_ssbd`, 3 of 3 runs, a mismatch at every exit), the write setting STIBP instead of SSBD (`plant_spec2_ssbd_wrong_bit`, mismatch at every exit), and the earlier IBRS-skip plant still convicted (regression). Host tests `tests/vmentry_spec2.rs` 12 (7 new), RED first against a stub that never decided SSBD or BHI (3 of 12 RED), GREEN; the SSBD plants are convicted by 3 and 1 host tests. COST (VM numbers, nested KVM, so cycles are upper bounds and noisy): the exit first step costs a mean of 1,160 cycles with SSBD and 1,163 without, the scene's wall time a mean of 120 s with and 124 s without: no measurable cost at this resolution; SSBD's real cost on bare metal is NOT measured. A BUG OF MINE found by the first the build VM run and fixed before landing: the scene's readback masked SPEC_CTRL with `& 1` and so read every exit as a mismatch once SSBD made the expected value 5; it now compares the bits the plan manages (IBRS, SSBD, BHI_DIS_S). (2) BHI (branch history injection; `BHI_DIS_S`, bit 10, CPUID.(7,2):EDX[4]) is set only on an eIBRS part without BHI_NO that enumerates BHI_CTRL; an eIBRS part without BHI_CTRL keeps naming BHI as uncovered and the software history-clear sequence is NOT built; a legacy-IBRS or BHI_NO part never gets the bit. This branch is HOST MODEL ONLY (the build VM has no BHI_CTRL; plant `plant_spec2_skip_bhi` is convicted by a host test and has no effect on the build VM). (3) RETPOLINE is NOT BUILT, and the reason is stated rather than hidden: between a VM exit and the next entry the host runs with IBRS set (shown by the readback above), so every host indirect branch in that window, for example the transition hook call, runs under IBRS and a retpoline would be redundant there; the only indirect branch that could run BEFORE IBRS is set would be in `synthyrion_vm_exit_prologue`, and the static gate counts zero (budget 0 met, 77 lines; the planted indirect call is convicted: `INDIRECT: call rax`); a CPU with no `IA32_SPEC_CTRL` is named as uncovered. A kernel-wide retpoline build is an owner decision (rustc 1.96 stable accepts `-Ctarget-feature=+retpoline-indirect-calls` only with a deprecation warning that becomes a hard error, and the sanctioned `-Zretpoline` is nightly-only); the default is not built. NOT COVERED, stated: the indirect branches on the DOMAIN SYSCALL and GATE path (in the `synthyrion_hal` release assembly 27 functions hold 63 indirect call or jump sites, none retpolined, including `synthyrion_syscall_rust` 11 and `synthyrion_gate_rust` 2) run with IBRS in whatever state ring-3 code left it; [2026-11-18: no longer true for the system-call and gate entries, which now write IBRS at every entry on a legacy-IBRS part; see the 2026-11-18 paragraph; the interrupt and exception entries remain uncovered] [2026-11-19: covered by unit SPECTRE-IDT] toggles IBRS at the VM transition only, so "retpolined or absent for the whole kernel" is NOT claimed. NOT SHOWN: live Spectre v4, branch-history or branch-target leakage is NOT demonstrated and no exploit or positive-control harness was built; silicon efficacy, including parts with enhanced IBRS or BHI_CTRL, stays under the hardware items. WHAT THIS UNIT FOUND, MEASURED: in 3 to 5 of every 10 two-CPU runs the audit reported one natural loss and re-queued it (item 5 runs r2two5, 6, 9, 10; r3tr2, 4; r4tr5, 6, 9; r6ship4, 7, 11; r6srec 5 of 10), yet with the re-queue SWITCHED OFF and no planted drop (`smp_plant_no_inject_recovery`, harness kind `smpnorecovery`) 44 of 44 runs reached LINUX-SCENE-OK (24 at the shipped preemption period, 20 at 8 times the exits: SYNTHYRION_PROBE_LINUX_SMP_PREEMPT_TICKS=16384) although 15 of them were flagged, and the end-of-run per-vector counters of a flagged run read `accepted 1, taken 1, EOI'd 1`. So the flagged events were NOT lost interrupts. THE MECHANISM, from the first-loss ring now printed with the audit: the guest took vector 0x24 on CPU 0 at its idle rip, ran its handler (about 30 exits of MMIO accesses at IF=0, with a self-IPI 0xfb nested), returned to IF=1 and issued the EOI for 0x24 a few exits LATER; the audit's premise (an interrupt-gate handler runs with IF clear until its EOI, so IF=1 with the vector in service and no EOI means it was never delivered) is false for such a guest, and the re-queue delivered a second copy of a vector the guest had already taken. THE FIX: a vector found in service at an IF=1 exit is a SUSPECT (`Vmm::injection_must_requeue`), re-queued only at a HLT exit (the 2026-11-05 failure: both CPUs parked with 0x30 in service) or after 2000 exits without its EOI; if the EOI arrives first it is counted as a late EOI and nothing is re-queued. Host test `a_suspect_vector_is_requeued_only_when_the_guest_parks_or_waits_past_the_horizon`: RED against a stub that never re-queues (1 of 13 failed), GREEN 13 of 13; `synthyrion_vmhost` all tests green; `boot_feature_sets` 12 of 12; `boot_stack_depth` 15 of 15; the scanner reports 0 findings on the 3 changed files. EVIDENCE ON THE FIX (tip 6f9d7dc11, every count a harness verdict on the build VM): TWO-CPU shipped configuration 20 of 20 PASS (r8ship1 to 15, r9ship16 to 20), 0 vectors re-queued, the suspects it saw (9 of 20 runs) were all EOI'd by the guest after at most 2 exits; STRESS (8 times the exits) 9 of 10 harness PASS plus 10 of 10 on the previous commit, 0 re-queued, late EOIs waited at most 4 exits; the one harness FAIL (r9str5) was captured whole and is a text-match artefact: the scene's own heartbeat line (written to COM1 every 50,000 exits) landed inside the guest's console line `smp: Brought up 1 node, 2 CPUs`, the scene's own checks were all true and it printed LINUX-SCENE-OK; PLANT GREEN 8 of 8 (the silent drop, 86 to 90 drops per run, every one detected and re-queued at the HLT, the run reaches OK), PLANT RED 2 of 2 (recovery off: no LINUX-SCENE-OK, the guest hangs); ONE-CPU 5 of 5; boots on the final tip: gate 66 of 66, root address space 49 of 49 with PREFLIGHT, release lean self-test PASS, AArch64 2 of 2. DISCLOSURES: (1) a first batch on the final tip (r8ship16 onward, 25 runs) was VOID, not a failure: the build VM's /tmp is a 7.9 GB tmpfs, the harness leaves a 24 MB image copy per run there and never removes it, 256 such directories filled it, the image copy of r8ship16 was cut off (18,513,920 bytes instead of 24,050,688) and every later copy was 0 bytes, so the guest never printed a byte; my own 179 directories were MOVED to disk (not deleted) and the 25 runs re-run as r9; (2) the full microkernel suite was not run for this unit: two other full suites were already running (the cap) and no microkernel or hal file is in the diff; (3) the root cause BELOW the guest (whether the nested host ever drops an injected event) is NOT proven and the 2026-11-05 hang was seen once and not seen again in 44 recovery-off runs, so what is shown is that the hang does not reproduce, that a drop is recovered (plant) and that the false re-delivery is gone; (4) a guest that re-enables interrupts inside a handler before its EOI and then HLTs with the vector still in service would be told the vector once more at that HLT; every re-queue is counted and printed. is NOT closed and NO confidentiality claim is made.

A mapping is a 32-byte record in a record frame, linked on its frame's chain (frame-table word w2); the writer and executor counters are frame-table word w3 and W^X (freeze, map writable or executable, table candidate) reads them; `o.fmaps` and `live_frame_maps` are REMOVED. The index (id -> slot) and the guards stay in the kernel table. The repair and the consistency test are the `GMap` functions themselves (`parts_consistent`, `parts_reseal`, `heal_parts`; a child guard is re-sealed in place by `GuardedVec::reload`, so no destructor), only the store differs. A frame word that is flipped, or that no field uses, makes the record read as ABSENT (fail closed) and the scrubber (implemented and tested, not yet run on a timer in the released image) rewrites the slot from the guards; a new context into a full table is refused (`Exhausted`); a destroyed context's slot is zeroed. Evidence: `framemap.rs` differential test against `GMap` (40,000 random operations, the full-table refusal exercised), every frame word and sampled bits of a slot flipped and repaired, a removed record leaves a zero slot; `synthyrion_kheal/tests/reload.rs`; plants RED (read without the consistency check, remove without the scrub, a refill dropped when stored, reload without the parity check); microkernel suite plain and fault_injection green. HOST only. **F6/F7-h item 1 (2026-11-01): a RETYPED context's record is IN the frame its region was charged for it**: `retype_in_for` places the 32 words at the first byte of the charged frame (`FMap::insert_at`, the address `FRAME_ARENA_BASE + start * FRAME_SIZE` through `real_phys`, as a notification's), so the charge HOLDS the state. A placement over another record's slot (two charges for one frame), over the table's own reserved block, for an id already present, or into a full table is REFUSED with nothing written (`Invalid` / `Exhausted`); destroying the context (its last capability, or the revoke of its region) scrubs all 32 words, and a store covers the whole slot, so nothing an earlier holder left survives. A frame corrupted behind the kernel's back reads as ABSENT (fail closed) and the scrubber repairs it from the guards; corrupting one context's frame does not reach another's. Evidence: `tests/sc_in_frame.rs` (3: the record read from the attached RAM at the charged frame over garbage, a flipped word not believed, the binding at word 4, the revoke leaving the frame zero, two contexts in two frames, a heap-created context in the reserved block), `framemap.rs` placement unit test; plants `plants_sc_in_frame.py` 6 of 6 RED (the record one frame away, every context at the first frame, no scrub on remove, a store that skips zero words, the overlap test dropped, a placement inside the reserved block accepted). HOST only. since F6/F7 gap 1 (2026-11-03) it takes ONE FRAME FROM THE FRAME ALLOCATOR (counted in use like any other, as `create_root_untyped` carves its region from it) and keeps its record there, no longer in a reserved block (the 4-frame reserved block is gone: `SHARE_TABLE_FRAMES` is the share table's frames alone); no allocator frame left is `Exhausted` and nothing is created; the frame is given back, scrubbed, only when the scrubber CONTAINS the record (a heap-created context has no destroy path, so it lives for the kernel's life otherwise); tests `sc_in_frame.rs` (the record at the first bytes of its allocator frame, a corrupted frame not believed, `frames_in_use` +1, the exhausted allocator) and `selfheal_aux.rs` (a contained heap-created context returns its frame); plants RED (`plants_sc_in_frame.py`: no allocator frame taken, the wrong refusal, the frame leaked on containment). `create_sched_context` itself stays callable until the seal; the guards (two check-word tables per context) and the id-to-frame index stay in the kernel's own storage, not in the frame. **F6/F7 item 2 (2026-11-02): that storage is now ALLOCATED ONCE AT CREATION and never grows** (the epoch table's pattern, `epochs.rs`): the index is a sorted array of `(id, pool slot)` pairs in two boxed arrays of `MAX_SCHED_CONTEXTS` entries, searched by a COUNTED binary search of at most `FMap::PROBES` (`floor(log2 CAP) + 1` = 7) probes, with the insert shifting up and the remove shifting down in COUNTED loops of at most CAP iterations; the guards are a pool of CAP records whose child-guard tables are created with the capacity of a context's refill array (`FrameRec::CHILD_WORDS`, `GuardedVec::with_capacity`), so re-sealing a refill list (`GuardedVec::reload`) never allocates. No `BTreeMap`, and no per-context allocation after creation, in the table. Measured by `synthyrion_wcetan` (bounds are MIR-statement counts from the host analysis, not cycles on hardware): BOUNDED now `FMap::find` 580, `next_id` 628, `overlaps_any` 3,663, `take` 8,611, `Sc::set_refill_words` 979, `Sc::scalar_words` 49, `set_scalar_words` 80, the slot codec `to_words` 1,715 and `from_words` 5,414 (the `Sc` scalar words were moved to uniquely named helpers because the analysis keys functions by readable name and the object header\'s `scalars` shared the name; the codec lost its `map_or` / `unwrap_or` / `min` combinators for `match`). HOST only.. A refill list longer than the array is REFUSED and nothing changes (the self-heal repair writes child lists back through `Rec::set_children`, which replaced `children_mut`; a guarded list that does not fit is a `Conflict`, never truncated); unit test + `selfheal_aux.rs` green on the old assertions; plants `plants_sc_fixed.py` 2 of 2 RED; HOST only; **F6/F7-g (2026-10-29): NOTIFICATIONS keep their state IN THEIR CHARGED FRAME.** The pending word, the bound task and the waiter queue's head and tail are four words at the start of the notification's own frame, read and written through an object-memory window (`objmem.rs`: the existing `PhysMem` trait the arch layer implements, or the host emulation when none is attached; `attach_object_memory` only before any notification exists and before the seal; a refused access is counted, `object_memory_faults`); the waiter queue is linked through the waiting tasks, so leaving it is O(1) and `forget_task_ipcx` no longer walks every notification, and `destroy_notification` walks the queue instead of the task table; the frame is zeroed when the notification is made and scrubbed when it is destroyed; the kernel keeps one index entry (notification id -> frame). `notification_invariants` checks every queue against the tasks. `tests/ntfn_in_frame.rs` (3) attaches its own RAM and reads and writes the frame behind the kernel's back; `heap_charge.rs` measures 572 B of live kernel heap per notification against 543 B per plain frame object (the region bookkeeping), the difference being the index entry; plants `plants_ntfn_in_frame.py` 7 of 7 RED. A behaviour change: a task that waits on a second notification leaves the first queue (the old lists kept a stale entry that a later signal woke spuriously), and a waiter of a destroyed notification now runs again (it was woken while still marked waiting, so it was never dispatched again; tested). HOST only. Still in kernel tables, stated at the mechanism: the task row and inbox, scheduling contexts (guarded `GMap`, self-heal), CNode slots, IO spaces, EPTs, VCPUs, and every object's header in `objs`; **F6/F7-d (2026-10-21): ANONYMOUS MEMORY names its region.** `map_anonymous_from(task, ut, virt, len)` and `cs_map_anonymous(task, ut_ptr, virt, len)` map fresh, zeroed, writable, never-executable pages whose frames are retyped Frame objects charged to the named untyped (all or nothing; a refusal, including one after the first chunk of frames was taken, maps and charges nothing; the region needs WRITE; revoking the region unmaps every page and frees every frame); `seal_object_creation` now closes `MapMemory` mode 0 (anonymous) and mode 3 (file; the page cache took allocator frames uncharged), so after the seal no frame leaves the allocator except through a region; and a request is at most `MAX_MAP_PAGES` (1024) pages: `len` comes from ring 3 and the range check and frame reservation were O(len) (a 2^40-page request was a ring-0 denial of service). 3 tests, 9 plants RED. **F6/F7-c (2026-10-21): REPLY SLOTS are part of their caller's task object.** A task has at most ONE outstanding call or fault (a call needs an idle, free-to-run caller; a fault needs a task that is not held; both hold it), so a reply slot is a fixed-size field of its caller, `TASK_OBJECT_BYTES` counts one `ReplySlot` per task and `ipc_invariants` states the bound (no caller has two slots, every slot belongs to a live held task, the table is never longer than the task table); an exhaustive sweep of 100,000 call / receive / reply / fault / restart / exit sequences over three tasks checks it after every operation and FOUND a real defect: `call` accepted a caller held by a fault, so a fault-suspended task could hold two slots (fixed: a caller must be free to run). the per-caller slot gives the same bound without changing the proved IPC model (see F13b). 2 tests, 4 plants RED. **F6/F7-b (2026-10-21): badged-endpoint table entries and signal slots are retyped too** (`retype_badged_endpoint(ut, from, badge, rights)`: the authority is a genuine UNBADGED source endpoint capability with GRANT, the new capability never exceeds the source's rights, a second retype of the same pair is a second object; `retype_signal_slot(ut, notif_obj, badge)`: the authority is WRITE on the notification OBJECT; each charged one frame, bounded by `MAX_BADGED_ENDPOINTS` / `MAX_SIGNAL_SLOTS` as a named `Exhausted`, destroyed with the region (the capability is then dead); a plain `retype` / `cs_retype` of the two kinds is refused because it could name any target; `seal_object_creation` closes `mint_badged_endpoint` and `sealed_signal_cap`; slot-path badged capabilities (`cs_mint`) are CapNodes accounted by their CNode and need no table; 4 tests, 12 plants RED) **F6/F7-a2 (2026-10-21): the task INBOX is charged with the task object**: it was an unbounded `VecDeque`, so the one frame a retyped task was charged covered nothing; now it has `INBOX_CAP` (32) incoming slots plus `MAX_OUTSTANDING_SENDS` (32) reserved bounce slots, is allocated once at `INBOX_SLOTS` and never grows, a full inbox REFUSES, a sender may have at most `MAX_OUTSTANDING_SENDS` word messages outstanding so every bounce finds the slot reserved for it, and a retyped TASK object is charged `TASK_OBJECT_FRAMES` (4: its row and its whole inbox at the largest delivery size, asserted by a test) instead of 1; 7 tests, 10 plants RED **F6/F7 gap 6 and the HAVE statement (2026-11-03).** (1) THE CHARGE NOW BOUNDS THE KERNEL HEAP FOR A FILLED CNODE (a real defect, found by measuring). F6/F7-f measured every kind retyped to exhaustion, but a CNode only EMPTY (its slots charged 40 B each). A CNode of 256 slots filled by copying one capability into every slot kept 166,700 B of kernel heap (653 B per occupied slot; 542 B at 16 slots, 572 B at 64) against the 12,288 B it was charged: 13.6 times what the region paid, and 14 to 15 times in the worst fill (a derivation chain, 720 B per slot, 192,732 B in all). The claim "the charge accounts for the heap" was FALSE for capability copies, derivations and mints, the operations that fill a CNode: a user holding one CNode could make the kernel hold far more than the user's memory paid for. Every slot is now charged FULL: `CAP_NODE_BYTES` 896 + `CNODE_EMPTY_SLOT_BYTES` 40 = `CNODE_SLOT_BYTES` 936, `cnode_frames` 59 at 256 slots [SUPERSEDED 2026-11-04 by the capability records in the CNode frames: now 48 + 40 + 128 = 216 per slot, 14 frames at 256; see below]. COST, stated: a CNode takes about 20 times the frames at 256 slots (59 against 3) and 4 times at 16 slots; the boot root CNode (64 slots, 15 frames) is kept back in the LARGEST boot region (`ROOT_CNODE_SLACK_FRAMES` = `cnode_frames(CNODE_MAX_BITS)` = 59 frames, `root_cnode_region`, `region_untyped_frames`) instead of one frame in every region; both live boots (x86 UEFI, AArch64) call `region_untyped_frames`. THE CHARGE MAY ONLY DROP LATER with a measured filled-CNode run proving held <= charged; the F29 U2 node-store move (capability records kept in the CNode's own slots) is the path to that, and `tests/heap_growth.rs` turns RED on purpose the moment capabilities become cheaper than half the constant, so the constant is lowered in the same unit that proves it. (2) EVIDENCE (HOST, the workspace's counting allocator): `tests/heap_growth.rs` fills CNodes of 4 to 256 slots by copy fan-out, by a derivation chain and by badged mints: the heap the retype and the fill leave live is at most the frames charged at every size (the tightest, 16 slots by chain, is 14,844 B of 16,384 B); the per-capability cost is at most `CAP_NODE_BYTES` and, for the chain, at least half of it (the charge is not slack); deleting every capability and refilling three times does not grow the heap; a task's inbox is allocated once, so sending until it is full and being refused allocates nothing; `bootmem.rs` boots a root CNode of 16, 64 and 256 slots with the slack `region_untyped_frames` keeps back and shows the old one-frame rule refused (`Exhausted`); Plants RED by name: `plants_heap_growth.py` (the old 40 B charge, `cnode_frames` ignoring the filled slots, a charge 6 times too large, a delete that leaves its node, an inbox that grows as messages queue, the old one-frame boot slack); `plants_heap_charge.py` re-pointed at the new constants. BOOT CHECKED IN QEMU TCG (VM, not bare metal): x86 UEFI under QEMU TCG, normal scenes: the default image (f17) 20/20, bootarm / BOOTROOT 23/23, bootinfo 12/12, gate -smp 1 65/65; AArch64 fast proof PASS (EL1 and EL2); the full AArch64 scenario sweep ran 25 min with no panic but did not complete, so there is no full-sweep verdict. (3) WHAT F6/F7 ARE, at HOST strength, and what is NOT shown. Held, measured: every kernel object after `seal_object_creation` is a retype charged to a region (model-checked, `synthyrion_kmath::untyped`); the kernel heap held for each retyped kind, for a filled CNode and for queued messages is at most the memory charged, and the revoke gives it back; reused memory is zeroed (obligation 2, model-checked); notifications and scheduling contexts keep their state in memory their region was charged for (a retyped scheduling context in its own frame, a heap-created one in one allocator frame), reply slots and the inbox are fixed parts of the task object. THE GAPS ARE EXECUTABLE (stage 1a, work item heap1_stage1a): `tests/heap_born_census.rs` holds one gate per kind; `a_task_is_not_heap_born` (a retyped task keeps 19,490 B of kernel heap against a 1,024 B index-entry budget) is `#[ignore]`d and UN-IGNORED BY proper (task row and inbox in the task object's frames), `a_cnode_is_not_heap_born` (a retyped 16-slot CNode keeps 1,486 B) BY the CapNode arena stages 2b and 2d; both print their measured number with `--ignored --nocapture`, and the notification gate is live. (b) the guards and the id index of the scheduling-context table are allocated once at creation in kernel memory, not in the frame; (c) in the live boots no `PhysMem` window is attached to the kernel (`attach_object_memory`), so notification and scheduling-context state sits in the object-memory emulation (kernel memory) there, and the in-frame property is host-tested with an attached window only; a VM scene that attaches an identity window over the kernel's regions and reads a retyped frame back is the missing evidence (it also needs the arch layer's `unsafe` window, the unsafe-confinement record and the boot-stack gate, and a fault in it breaks the default boot, so it is not done in a release-day unit); (d) heap growth from operations on tables other than the ones measured here (frame mappings, the page-table model, shares) is bounded by their own limits and is NOT measured against a charge; (e) **CORRECTED 2026-11-10: this sentence is no longer true.** The host object-memory emulation is a bounded array (`objmem::Emu`, EMU_WORDS 29,968 words, a binary search of EMU_PROBES 15 probes) with loop rows for `Emu::lower` and the `Emu::set` insert and remove shifts in `docs/kernel/wcet/wcet_loop_bounds.tsv`, and the generated `docs/kernel/KERNEL_WCET_TABLE.md` on main d978cae93 names `ObjRam::peek` / `write` as the first thing in the way of NO entry (its root causes are now `AsidPool::new`, `<usize as From<u16>>::from`, `BTreeSet::contains`, `BTreeMap::remove` / `range`, `RingMem::read`, `Iterator::enumerate` and similar); `synthyrion_wcetan check` reports 0 problems with the table current (the stage of a queued submission ACCEPTED it in 991 s). What is STILL open in the WCET table is exactly the 29 `PENDING` kernel entries for those other reasons (unbounded maps, allocation, loops with no capacity), among them `retype`, `cnode_copy`, `cnode_lookup` and the two scrubbers `selfheal_Kernel__cap_scrub` / `obj_scrub` (their loops now have a source-constant ceiling, below, but their per-unit lookups are over tables with no enforced capacity). Strength: HOST static analysis, in MIR statements, not cycles. LOCAL SELF-CHECKS RUN before the direct enqueue: the microkernel suite with `fault_injection` at green; `synthyrion_gatecases` and `synthyrion_vmhost` green; plants 27 of 27 RED (`plants_heap_growth.py` 6, `plants_sc_gap.py` 5, `plants_sc_in_frame.py` 12, `plants_heap_charge.py` 4) and `plants_sc_rows.py` 5 of 5 earlier; NOT run locally: the census-check and the full `synthyrion_kmath` suite on the final tree (its census test is regenerated at landing). **F6/F7 (2026-11-04): BADGED-ENDPOINT ENTRIES ARE IN THEIR CHARGED FRAMES.** The badged-endpoint table is no longer a kernel `BTreeMap`: it is `FMap<PairRec, MAX_BADGED_ENDPOINTS>` (`pairrec.rs`, `framemap.rs`), the fixed-capacity guarded frame table the scheduling contexts use. A retyped badged endpoint keeps its two words (target task, badge) at the first bytes of the frame its retype charged (the rest of the slot is written zero, so no word of an earlier holder survives); one made before the seal takes ONE allocator frame it owns and gives back, scrubbed, when the entry goes; the table's index and guards are allocated once at kernel creation. The withdrawal (`revoke_badged_of`) finds a target's entries by the target word (the frame's, or the guarded copy's when the frame is damaged) into a fixed list and removes them without checking, so a damaged record cannot keep an endpoint alive; a corrupted record reads as absent (fail closed). MEASURED (the workspace counting allocator, HOST): 256 heap-created endpoints leave 0 B of kernel heap live; 256 retyped endpoints then revoked leave 0 B on the second round (5,120 B once, the object tables' first growth). Tests: `badged_in_frame.rs` (5), the codec tests in `pairrec.rs` (2), a differential test that the frame table's consistency test equals the generic one; 10 plants RED (`plants_badged_in_frame.py`). WCET: `FMap::get` is costed, so `send_caps` stays BOUNDED (1,999,967 -> 2,066,277 MIR statements, one frame-table get), `synthyrion_wcetan check` 0 problems; the frame table's rows are stated at its largest instantiation (256 entries, 9 probes, 16 child words), and the table's own consistency test is a bounded function because the generic one walks an object header's unbounded child list (114 M statements). Not yet in frames: signal slots (unit 3b, queued), the task row and inbox, CNode capability nodes (arena 2a-2d), IO spaces, EPTs, VCPUs, `objs` headers. Strength: HOST. A retyped slot keeps its two words (notification, badge) at the first bytes of the frame its retype charged; one made before the seal (`sealed_signal_cap`) takes ONE allocator frame it owns and gives back, scrubbed, with the slot. A notification's death takes every slot that raises it (found by the notification word into a caller-owned fixed list, in a non-inlined function, so no caller's stack frame grows) and the interrupt lines that signalled through them; a corrupted record reads as absent (fail closed).
